       nothing ever wrote them down they went on reading "up" indefinitely, which is the one thing
       they certainly were not.
       SEEN: the cap taken from 30 to 10 with devices on slots 12 and 25. The group device read
       "5/7 up" beside NINE member devices in the list, two of them showing up and neither heard
       from since the cap changed. Both numbers were right about the seven slots the loop could
       see and said nothing at all about the other two, which is why it looked like a counting bug
       and was not one.
       COUNTED, AND PUT DOWN. Counted because they are still this group's devices and the second
       number is meant to be the one you would otherwise get by counting the list by hand -
       leaving them out is exactly what made the device and the list disagree. Put down because a
       device nothing tracks is down by definition. The six kind counters and numberOfUp still
       close on the new total, because the stranded ones are tallied the same way everything else
       is.
       NOT DELETED, and not renumbered into a free slot: deleting is not this plugin's habit, and
       moving a device to another slot would silently point it at a different tag. A level 20 line,
       once an hour, names them and says which number to raise to get them back - at which point
       they resume with mac, tagIds, previousMacs and history all intact.
       Also in findMyMemberDevices(): two member devices claiming the SAME slot, or one with no
       slot at all, were dropped on the floor by the lookup with no error and no log. Neither is
       repaired automatically - guessing which of two devices owns a slot would be worse than
       saying so - but both are now reported once an hour with names and ids.
   FIND MY: the rotation matcher now knows WHEN a mac rotation is due. A find my address is not
       replaced at an arbitrary moment - it changes on a 15 minute grid aligned to the unix epoch,
       IN BOTH MODES, which is what the key schedule behind these addresses would produce.
       MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED - 69 hours on two rpis, 155 usable mac lives, with findmy_rotation.py.
       Births taken mod 900 clustered at R=0.851 over 73 nearby macs at a phase of +12s, and 0.908
       over 14 seen in both modes at +35s. THE ROW THAT MAKES IT REAL IS 1800: a 1800 or 3600
       second grid is also a 900 second grid, so a high score at 900 alone proves nothing - but
       the same macs scored 0.137 at both 1800 and 3600, so the births land on the 900 lattice and
       on no multiple of it. The nearby arm also carried it: 55 of 72 lives straddled 900s exactly.
       WHERE IT ACTS is the pairwise tie - a lineage claimed by two newcomers within ROTTIE, ie
       the module saying signal cannot tell them apart. Until now that ended with the lineage
       BURNT FOR BOTH and nobody getting it. When one of them appeared on the clock and the other
       in the middle of a slot, the mid-slot one now steps aside, the same move the "rival is
       clearly closer" case already makes. One of them having it beats neither, when there is a
       reason to prefer one. Only at the two ends of the ramp - past ROTGRIDFAR against inside
       ROTGRIDNEAR - because the measured spread is about 81s against a 900s period and separates
       a boundary from mid-slot, nothing finer.
       WHAT IT MUST NOT DO, and the first cut of this did it: rotBatchSolve() forces a pair when
       breaking it costs more than ROTBATCH extra, so anything added to that cost moves the margin
       - and a clock penalty on the newcomer SIGNAL FAVOURED subtracts from the very margin about
       to force it. A pairing 6 dBm ahead, forced before, stopped being forced once a 3 point
       penalty ate into its lead: the clock made a correct answer less certain. Caught by its own
       test before it went anywhere. In the wave solve the clock is now worth ROTGRIDTIE = 0.001
       of a dBm, which separates arrangements costing exactly the same - where the solver used to
       pick by iteration order - and can never reach ROTBATCH.
       ROTGRIDPEN is deliberately equal to ROTTIE, so the clock can break a tie and can never
       overturn what signal settled, and it is far below ROTTYPEPEN and ROTMODEPEN, so an apple
       type mismatch still outranks it absolutely. New log line ROTB~ prints where each newcomer
       in a wave fell on the clock, and only when at least one was off it.
       Tested: 19 cases - the regression above in both directions, the ramp including the far side
       of a boundary and an unreadable birth, the elif chain the new branch sits in (both on the
       clock, both mid-slot, the clock pointing our way, half-way up the ramp, and a rival clearly
       closer, which reaches the earlier branch and never consults the clock), and that none of
       the weights can be traded against another.
   FIND MY: "max number of member devices" now says how to pick a number instead of only warning
       what happens when it is too low. Count the find my devices you expect in range - yours AND
       the neighbours', everything above the join level - and add 2 to 10.
       WHY THE HEADROOM IS NOT SPARE: when a mac rotation is not matched the new mac takes a fresh
       slot while the old one is still held for the tag that has just gone, so one tag occupies
       two slots until the old device has been down long enough to be handed on. The list
       therefore sits a little above the number of tags actually present as a matter of course,
       and settles back by itself - which reads as a fault if nobody says so first. Same guidance
       in the tooltip and in the forum post, where it is now one of only three things to set.
   SHUTDOWN: the two background thread loops now catch self.StopThread instead of swallowing it,
       which is both the traceback logged on every plugin stop and, almost certainly, "I have to
       copy a new version to the server twice and wait 5 seconds before restarting".
       HOW INDIGO STOPS A THREAD is by raising StopThread out of self.sleep(). It derives from
       Exception, so a generic "except Exception: log the traceback" catches it, reports a normal
       shutdown at level 40 as though something had broken, AND GOES ROUND THE LOOP AGAIN. The
       thread never exited; indigo waited out its shutdown timeout and killed the process. A copy
       of a new plugin.py was therefore racing a stop that had not finished, which is exactly the
       shape of "do it twice and it works".
       workOnBeaconMessages was the one in the report. delayedActionsThread was worse: its handler
       logged the traceback and then called self.sleep(3), which raised StopThread straight back
       out of the except block, so one shutdown logged the same traceback twice. Both break out of
       their loop now and exit within one sleep interval, 0.05s and 0.5s.
       ORDER OF EXCEPT CLAUSES IS THE WHOLE FIX: the named handler has to come first, because a
       handler for Exception swallows it wherever it is reached.
       STILL OUTSTANDING, not changed here: 25 other try-blocks around a self.sleep() swallow
       StopThread the same way. They are helpers rather than loops, so they return instead of
       spinning, and the thread loop above them now exits cleanly on its next sleep - but a stop
       landing inside one of them can still log a single spurious traceback on the way out.
       deviceStopComm was checked and is already correct: it skips its sleep when pluginState is
       "stop", so it does not do this once per device during shutdown.
   FIND MY: the group dialog above the EXPERTS ONLY line is now the whole setup and nothing else -
       which rPis count, the join/leave levels, and the max number of member devices. Everything
       that follows moved below it: when a member goes down, when its slot may be reused, the
       once-a-day tidy and its log-destination toggle, which was the last logging setting left
       above the line. "most member devices" is called "max number of member devices". The
       housekeeping settings kept the delete option and its logging toggle together, because that
       checkbox only modifies the setting directly above it and splitting them helps nobody.
   FIND MY: the seeding of a newly added rPi's states is done on the UPDATE path as well, because
       the pass that did it can arrive too early. refreshFindMyMemberStates runs about 2s after
       the group is saved, and indigo does not necessarily have the new states on the device by
       then - observed directly, the states appeared a noticeable while after the save. A loop
       that cannot see Pi_05_Signal yet does not seed it, and nothing ever came back: the device
       was then left reading 0 dBm and 0.0 m for good, ie "on top of that rPi at full strength"
       for an rPi that has never heard the tag.
       Now every message checks it, so it heals within a minute however the timing falls. One
       write per state, once - afterwards the test is false for ever, a tag that has really been
       heard has a Pi_NN_Time and is never touched, and a faint reading is written as itself
       rather than seeded. Tested with both branches together, including that precedence.
   FIND MY: an rPi that hears a tag too faintly to count now SHOWS that reading. The rPi has
       always sent every mac it hears but does not count, and this side has always used it to say
       Pi_NN_State "weak" - and then wrote no number, so a member device read "weak" beside a
       signal of -999: "that rPi can hear this tag" and "that rPi has never heard this tag", on
       two lines of the same device. Seen on a live two-rPi group, slot 10.
       WHAT THE NUMBER IS GOOD FOR is the one thing a bare "weak" cannot say: HOW far below the
       join level. -88 against a join of -80 is a threshold worth reconsidering; -95 is a tag on
       the far side of the house. Both printed as -999.
       Pi_NN_Time is stamped with it, which also keeps the invariant the new seeding pass depends
       on - a non-empty Pi_NN_Time means that rPi has heard this tag, member or not - and -999 now
       means strictly "never heard by any means", which is what the dialog always claimed.
       IT DOES NOT COMPETE FOR closestRPI, deliberately. A weak reading can be numerically the
       stronger of the two, because membership carries hysteresis from join down to leave, and a
       tag held at -90 by an rPi that counts it should not be moved to one reading -85 that does
       not. Tested: 13 cases, including that trap, plus noData and reporting-but-deaf, which still
       write nothing at all so the last real reading survives.
   FIND MY: adding an rPi to an existing group left every member device claiming that rPi was
       hearing the tag at full strength. createFindMyMemberDevice() stamps -999 / 999 / "down"
       over the states a device has AT BIRTH, and that was the only place it was done - so a
       Pi_NN_ trio that appears LATER, which is exactly what ticking a new rPi on the group does
       to every member that already existed, stayed at indigo's type defaults of 0 and 0.0. A
       signal of 0 dBm and a distance of 0.0 m read as "on top of that rPi", which is the opposite
       of the truth: that rPi has never heard the tag at all.
       SEEN DOING IT on a two-rPi group: rPi 5 added to members dating from two days earlier, all
       of them reading Pi_05_Signal 0 and Pi_05_Distance 0.0, sitting beside slots created after
       the change that correctly read -999 and 999.
       Seeded now on every group save and every plugin start, where the rest of the member-device
       housekeeping already lives. THE TEST IS Pi_NN_Time AND NOT THE VALUE: it is written only
       when that rPi really did report this tag, so an empty one is the unambiguous "never heard
       from it", and a tag that has gone out of range keeps the last reading it left behind.
       The existing distance-of-0 repair also counted only -999 as "never measured" and so skipped
       these devices entirely, since their signal was 0 rather than -999. It now counts both.
   FIND MY: the calibration csv is written STRAIGHT TO THE CARD and the cap is 20MB, about 40
       hours, instead of 200kB in temp/, about 25 minutes. It survives a reboot by itself.
       THE OLD ARRANGEMENT COST MORE THAN IT SAVED. The file lived on the 2MB tmpfs to spare the
       card the 10 second appends, and was copied out every PERSISTSECS - but it copied the WHOLE
       file every time one row had been added, so a 200kB log wrote 200kB to the card 432 times a
       day, about 86MB, to carry 12MB of actual rows. Writing the rows where they belong writes
       those 12MB and nothing else: SEVEN TIMES LESS card traffic, in more writes of much smaller
       size. The bytes are what wears a card, and they went down.
       AND IT IS WHAT MAKES A USEFUL CAP POSSIBLE, which is the reason for doing it. In temp/ the
       log was bounded by a ram disk it had to share with findMyGroup.state - past a point it
       would have taken the state file down with it - so 200kB was not a tuning choice. On the
       card the bound is the card, and 40 hours costs nothing. The questions this file answers,
       from where to put join/leave to whether nearby and separated rotate on one clock, are all
       measured over hours; 25 minutes answered none of them and meant pulling it on a loop.
       restoreCsv(), persistCsvFile() and csvStamp() are gone with the reason for them, as is
       findMyGroup.csv from master's keep-list for temp/. The state file is unchanged: still
       written to temp/ every send and persisted every PERSISTSECS.
       THE COST, on the record: appends go from 432 a day to 8640, while the log is on. It could
       be buffered in memory and flushed on the PERSISTSECS tick if that ever matters - it is not,
       because the file is read live during a calibration walk and one that is up to 200s behind
       what the rPi is hearing is a different tool.
       Tested offline: the file lands in the pibeacon dir and not in temp/, two restarts APPEND to
       it (30 -> 61 -> 92 rows) rather than restoring or truncating, wiping temp/ costs it
       nothing, the trim still caps and still writes the header back at the top, and the state
       file still uses temp/ and still persists.
   FIND MY: new mac-side tool findmy_rotation.py, beside findmy_merge.py in Server Plugin/. It
       answers one question off the calibration csv, with no keys and no rPi change: does a mac in
       NEARBY mode rotate on the same clock as one in SEPARATED mode. That matters because the
       separated payload carries the tag's rotating public key and the BLE address is the first 6
       bytes of it - so IF the address comes off the same schedule whatever the tag is
       broadcasting, a precomputed list of a tag's future macs identifies it exactly, in both
       modes, and none of the rotation matching is needed for tags whose keys you hold.
       IT MEASURES BIRTHS, NOT LIFETIMES, because that is what separates the two cases: a mac's
       birth time is taken mod a candidate period and the clustering measured as the mean
       resultant length R - 1.0 is every birth at the same phase, 0.0 is spread evenly round the
       period, and evenly spread is exactly what "not on this clock" looks like. A plain mean
       cannot say it: 1s and 899s are 2 seconds apart on a 900 second circle.
       TRUE BIRTH COMES FROM age_s, which is why that column earns its place - it is written as
       "now - first heard", so a mac's birth is recoverable from any row it appears in, including
       the first row of a file that trimCsv() has already dropped the start of. Without it every
       mac alive when logging began would look newborn.
       THE TWO WAYS R LIES ARE MARKED ON THE LINE rather than left to be noticed: it is 1.0 for a
       single sample whatever the period, and near 1.0 for any period longer than the observation
       window. Both print the reason beside the number. Validated on synthetic 12h data: six
       nearby tags on the 15-minute grid read R=1.000 at the same phase as the separated ones,
       and the same tags with arbitrary birth times read R=0.065.
       PASSERS-BY ARE FILTERED OUT, which the first live run showed was not optional: lifetimes of
       1 and 2 seconds were sitting in the distribution beside real rotations. A neighbour's tag
       drifting past at -95 has a "life" and a "birth" and neither is a rotation, and left in they
       flatten the lifetimes towards zero and push R towards 0 for EVERY mode equally - which is
       the same shape as the answer "nearby is not on this clock" and would be read as it. A mac
       now has to have been heard over at least 3 rows, for at least 60s, for at least 70% of its
       observed span. The dropped ones are counted and split by reason rather than vanishing.
       COVERAGE IS MEASURED PER RPI AND THE BEST ONE WINS. Two negative controls were needed to
       get this right. Counting rows against a cadence measured over the whole input was wrong the
       moment two rPis were given at once - they do not report in step, their rows interleave, the
       global gap collapses and a tag heard continuously by both scored 20%. And taking a mac's
       own MEDIAN gap as the baseline was wrong for a tag heard in three brief bursts, where half
       the gaps are holes and the median is itself a hole. It is the SMALLEST gap on one rPi that
       says how often that rPi reports, which is what a hole has to be measured against.
       A LIFETIME HISTOGRAM in 60s buckets is printed as well, because it works on far less data
       than R does - R needs the window to cover the period several times over, while a pile of
       lives sitting on one value shows up with a dozen of them. It does NOT answer the nearby
       question though, and the controls prove it: the histogram is IDENTICAL in both, a clean
       pile at 840-899s either way. Only the birth phase separates them.
       THE HISTOGRAM'S LAST BUCKET IS AN OVERFLOW AND NOW SAYS SO. It folded everything at or
       above the cap into the top 60s bucket under that bucket's own label, which did not merely
       lose detail - it INVENTED A PILE. On the first long run, separated lives of 3797, 4293,
       4988 and 11767s printed as "5 macs at 3540-3599s", which reads as a tight cluster at 59
       minutes and is the exact thing the histogram exists to show. Anything past the cap is
       labelled ">= 3600s" now and carries its own range and median, so a wide spread cannot be
       read as a period again.
       A DEATH IS NOW CLASSIFIED AS A ROTATION OR A DEPARTURE, which the second live run forced:
       three separated macs had lifetimes of 249-264s against a 15 minute rotation, with perfect
       coverage. They were tags heard continuously and then walking OUT OF RANGE - a real,
       well-measured lifetime that is not a rotation interval, and a death that is not a rotation
       boundary. The coverage filter cannot touch that case: nothing about it is intermittent.
       A rotation has a SUCCESSOR - the tag does not stop advertising, it changes address, so the
       old mac's last packet and the new mac's first are all but simultaneous, which is the whole
       basis of the matching in findMyGroup.py. A departure has none. Lives that ended in a
       rotation carry the lifetimes and the histogram; births that BEGAN in one carry the birth
       phase; the rest are counted and set aside. Nothing tries to say which mac succeeded which -
       with several tags in the house that cannot be done from timing - only whether a partner
       exists, which is enough in bulk. The pairing rate is printed as the check on it.
       --succ-gap defaults to 30s rather than findMyGroup's ROTGAP of 15: that one judges frames,
       this judges a csv sampled every logEverySecs, where a handover that really took 0s can be
       observed two rows apart.
       AND THE TOOL NO LONGER EATS ITS OWN OUTPUT. "findmy_rotation.py *.csv --out rotation.csv"
       feeds the previous run's rotation.csv straight back in; its rows all failed to parse and
       were dropped one at a time, so the header said "files: 2" over one file's worth of rows.
       An --out file is now recognised and skipped by name, and the row count is printed PER FILE.
   FIND MY: the group dialog's debug, calibration and print settings, and the "create a device
       per member" checkbox, are all below the EXPERTS ONLY line now. Everything a regular user
       sets is above it: which rPis count, the join/leave levels, and the member device limits.
       "create a device per member" is ON by default, so the only reason to look at it is to turn
       it OFF - which is exactly the test for what belongs below the line. The two print buttons
       now sit together in the logging section instead of one being at the top and one at the
       bottom. The EXPERTS heading says what is under it: tuning that can quietly drift the tag
       identities, and logging that costs nothing and changes no behaviour.
   FIND MY: the group device now says HOW MANY TAGS ARE IN EACH ROOM - one Pi_NN_Tags state per
       rPi it is set to use, holding how many member devices that rPi is nearest to right now.
       This is the number worth automating on. Counting is the one thing this feature gets right:
       an individual member device is a SLOT, and if a mac rotation goes unmatched it describes a
       different tag under the same name - but "three find my devices are in the kitchen" is
       correct whichever tag is which. Until now that could only be asked by walking the member
       devices from a script, ie by asking the devices that are explicitly not to be trusted one
       at a time; now it is a state on the group and a trigger needs no script.
       COUNTED FROM closestRPI AND NOT FROM THE UNION, which matters more than it sounds: that
       state has already had the moveHoldSecs damping applied, so a tag sitting on the boundary
       between two rPis does not hand back and forth - and a trigger on these counts would have
       fired on exactly that flicker. A member inside its down-delay keeps its room too, so the
       room counts hold a tag for as long as numberOfUp does.
       TALLIED AT THE SAME THREE POINTS AS THE AIRTAG/AIRPODS SPLIT, from the same up/down
       decision, so they add up to numberOfUp instead of being worked out separately and drifting
       from it. Only members that are UP are counted - a tag that has gone quiet is in no room.
       An enabled rPi that hears nothing writes 0, which is a real answer and the one a room
       emptying needs. Only the rPis ticked on the group get the state at all, and saving the
       group rebuilds its own state list as well as its members'.
   FIND MY: "create a device per member" is ON by default now, in the dialog and in the code that
       reads it. A group device is far more useful with a device per tag than as a bare count, and
       that is what almost everybody wants from it - having to find and tick a checkbox before the
       feature does the visible half of its job was the wrong way round. Untick it for counts only.
       The code fallback was False while the dialog default was being changed to True, which would
       have left a group device whose props predate the field behaving opposite to a new one; both
       say True now, so such a device starts creating member devices exactly as a new one does.
   FIND MY: the "phone/mac" kind and the findmy_NN_phone-mac name are gone. The branch fired on
       nearby-info (10) or handoff (0C) and labelled the device a phone or a mac - but an iphone or
       a mac in normal use is NEVER counted here: only a device that is itself LOST broadcasts the
       find my payload, and one on the network RELAYS other people's tags instead of advertising
       itself. Nothing reaches the naming code without having sent find my, so the label was
       claiming a kind that cannot be present, and it named devices on the strength of it. Such a
       mac now reads "findmy+other", which says it sends more than find my without inventing what
       it is. findMyMemberNameIsOurs() still recognises the retired "phone-mac" suffix, so a device
       already carrying that name is renamed rather than mistaken for a name somebody chose and
       left frozen for ever. The same wrong claim is out of the group device's dialog text, which
       now says plainly that phones and macs do not appear and why.
   FIND MY: a stall in the feed no longer empties the room. A mac is expired after expireSecs
       without a packet, which is right when THAT mac stopped - but "last" only moves when a frame
       is DELIVERED, so a stall in the handoff expired EVERY mac at once while all of them were
       still there, and the count fell to zero and climbed back. Measured 2026-09-27: 23:F4 absent
       from five consecutive summaries and back with 62 packets in ONE row, the radio having heard
       it throughout; and 20:23:18, eleven seconds with nothing from anybody, one second past
       expireSecs for the whole house. The rotation half of this was already handled - newcomers
       park and claims wait for the feed, see GAPGRACE - this is the COUNT.
       WHEN NOTHING AT ALL HAS BEEN HEARD nothing has been learned about any one tag, so the expiry
       pass is skipped entirely: the one thing every mac shares is the feed. A mac's silence is only
       evidence while OTHER macs are being heard through it, which is why one mac going quiet among
       eleven that are reporting still expires exactly as before.
       BOUNDED AT FEEDTRUST, 120 s, because a dongle that has genuinely died is also silent and must
       end up reporting an empty room rather than a full one for ever - and it says so in the log
       when it gives up. AND IT TAKES FEEDMINMACS, 2, TO INFER ANYTHING AT ALL: with one mac in
       range its silence and the feed's silence are the same event, so it expires on time as it
       always did. Nothing is reset while held - "last" and the member flags are untouched, so the
       macs that come back need no re-joining, and anything that really went expires on the first
       pass after the feed returns. New log lines ROTH (holding), ROTH= (feed back) and ROTH! (gave
       up, check the radio), all at level 20.

v2022.194.7  @ 2026-09-28
   FIND MY: a whole WAVE of rotations is now solved as one problem, not as N separate contests.
       Tags rotate together: measured 2026-09-27, EIGHT of the twelve macs on one rpi were born
       inside a single ten second window, and then every newcomer is a rival of every other for the
       same handful of lineages. Of the 28 pairs in that wave, 8 shared a battery word and sat inside
       ROTRSSI of each other and 4 of those were inside ROTTIE, so the arbitrations came down to 1, 1
       and 4 dBm - on a smoothed signal that wanders a median of 4.8 dBm for a mac that has not moved
       (range 1.5 to 8.0 over 12 macs, same capture). The contested branch also burns a lineage for
       claims that never contested it, which removes the very evidence a wave needs.
       rotBatchSolve() takes the bipartite graph resolveRotClaims() already builds - every claim in
       flight against its candidates that are still silent - and asks which WHOLE assignment is best,
       most pairs first and then cheapest, with an exact apple-type match outranking a subset by more
       than any dBm difference can cross. A pairing that looks poor alone is often the only way the
       rest of the wave works out, so solved together the answer is frequently unique where no single
       pair was. Then every pair in it is put to the test: forbid just that pair, solve again, and
       only if the best remaining answer explains fewer tags - or costs ROTBATCH more - is the pair
       believed. Per pair and not per assignment, so a wave where the near tags separate and the far
       ones do not commits the near ones and lets the far ones wait.
       IT IS ADDITIVE ON PURPOSE AND THAT WAS FOUND BY TESTING, not designed in: ROTBATCH (5 dBm, the
       measured noise) is STRICTER than ROTTIE (3), so a wave solve that refused whatever it could not
       force would throw away pairings the pairwise path gets right. Replayed against the real 13:30
       wave, 4C:98 -> BB:AF was 4 dBm against 0 for its rival - ROTTIE settles that correctly and a
       5 dBm margin will not. So the wave only ever ADDS a commit, and changes the pairwise path in
       exactly two places: inside a wave a contested lineage is no longer BURNT for everyone else, and
       a mac the wave cannot place yet waits up to ROTAMBIG before minting a lineage instead of
       minting immediately. Both are strictly later, never different.
       AND THE WAVE THAT PROMPTED THIS WAS RESOLVED CORRECTLY, which is the honest state of the
       evidence: every candidate that wave turned down was still alive and came back or rotated later,
       so the two macs that took fresh tagIds really were new tags. This is not fixing an observed
       wrong answer, it is removing a way of arriving at one - 1 dBm is not evidence on a signal that
       wanders 4.8, however the arithmetic comes out.
       Bounded at BATCHMAXN 8 newcomers by BATCHMAXM 12 lineages, a bitmask DP over the lineages so
       it is 2^M * N rather than M! - beyond that the pairwise path runs as before and says so. New
       log lines ROTB (the wave and what it forced), ROTB= (a forced pairing committed), ROTB~ (a mac
       waiting on a wave) and ROTB? (a wave too large to solve).

v2022.194.6  @ 2026-09-28
   FIND MY: the "all NN slots are taken and .. is new - slot NN is handed over to it" line goes to
       plugin.log only (level 10). indigo_log_handler sits at INFO, so 20 put one of these in the
       indigo event log for every hand-over - and since .194.3 a hand-over is the NORMAL way a slot
       is filled rather than an incident. Nothing is destroyed by one, so there is nothing in it the
       event log needs to interrupt anybody with.
       SAME FOR "slot NN renamed .. from everything it has been heard sending", for the same reason:
       it fires on every slot that changes KIND, and a slot changing hands is ordinary now - an
       airpods case's device reused by an airtag is renamed the moment the types are relearned. It
       only ever renames a name this plugin gave itself, so nothing of the user's is touched by it.
       WHAT STILL REACHES THE EVENT LOG is what is either news or asked for: a numbering that
       restarted (the runId void), an rPi still on an older findMyGroup.py, the one-off state-column
       repair, the "take over" menu action and "print members". The deletion line in
       findMyDeleteOldMembers() is still 20 by default, and still switchable with "log deletions to
       plugin.log only".

v2022.194.5  @ 2026-09-28
   FIND MY: "the state file is lost at every restart" is FIXED, and the state file was never lost.
       THE RESTORE WAS BEING THROWN AWAY. __init__ sets enabled False, calls reset(), then
       restoreSlots() - which correctly brings back nextTagId, the slot map, tagIdOf, ownTag and the
       runId. Then beaconloop's first readParams() reaches setParams(), enabled goes False -> True,
       and "if self.enabled != wasEnabled: self.reset()" wiped every bit of it seconds later.
       restoreSlots() was never called a second time, so nextTagId was 1 on EVERY start, every
       lineage was re-minted, and each restart looked to indigo like a houseful of new devices.
       That is why chasing the FILE never found anything - readState handles a missing and a
       truncated temp file, and on the rPi that produced this the file was intact the whole time:
       the pi had not rebooted, so temp/ was never emptied, and master spares findMyGroup.state from
       its wipe by name in any case.
       IT NEEDS NO RESTART EITHER, which is what made it look mysterious. makeParametersFile() sets
       out["findMyGroup"] = {"enable":"0"} FIRST and overwrites it only when it finds an enabled
       group device for that rPi, so any push that does not find one writes "off" - and the rPi then
       flips enabled True -> False -> True over two pushes and resets twice, with nothing having
       restarted. Measured on one afternoon with master restarting several times and the pi not
       restarting at all: tagIds re-numbered at 15:51, 18:21 and 19:13, previousTagIds in the
       2-20..2-29 range beside current ones of 2-1..2-9, the same lineage id on two devices at once,
       one mac on two slots, and three slots trading macs in a ring.
       THE FIX IS TO READ IT BACK after the reset, and only when the group is now ON: going the
       other way the reset is the whole point and there is nothing to come back to. Switching off and
       on again restores too, which is right rather than incidental - the indigo devices did not go
       anywhere while it was off, so their slots and lineages are still the truth. Verified offline
       for both paths: the enable flap keeps nextTagId, the runId, the slot map and the lineages, and
       so does a real restart with the state file intact.
       WITH THIS, THE runId GUARD OF .194.4 SHOULD NEVER FIRE ON A HEALTHY rPi. It stays as the net:
       it is what makes a numbering that really did restart harmless instead of silently wrong, and
       it is how this was caught at all.

v2022.194.4  @ 2026-09-28
   FIND MY: the rPi now says WHICH NUMBERING its tagIds belong to, and that closes the hole the
       counter test of .194.1 could not. A tagId is worth something only because it is never reused;
       the rPi mints them from a counter in its state file, so when that file cannot be read the
       counter restarts at 1 and hands out ids the plugin still remembers as OTHER tags. The test
       shipped that afternoon compared the counter against the ids the devices held - correct, and
       only good for the FIRST restart: after one loss every stored id is small, so the next loss
       climbs past them within minutes and looks like ordinary progress. Measured 2026-09-27 21:35,
       with that guard live: tagId 2-3 on slots 02 and 09, 2-4 on slots 04 and 05, three devices
       re-numbered at 19:13 colliding with two numbered at 15:51 and 18:21.
       So findMyGroup mints a runId whenever its counter restarts - in reset(), OVERWRITTEN by
       restoreSlots() when there is a state file to restore, which makes it change on exactly the
       event that matters: a clean restart keeps its numbering and its counter together, a lost
       state file gets a new one. It travels in every message beside nextTagId. The plugin voids
       ALL of that rPi's stored lineages when it changes - not the ones above some number, because
       an id of 2 from the old numbering and an id of 2 from the new one are different tags and no
       arithmetic separates them - and it remembers the runId in the GROUP DEVICE'S OWN PROPS rather
       than only in memory, so a plugin restart does not forget which numbering the member devices
       came from. That window is exactly when both sides restart together. Written only when it
       changes, so it is not a props write on the message path. The counter test is kept as the
       backstop for an rPi still on an older findMyGroup.py, and is skipped when a runId arrives.
       THE ID IS RANDOM, not the clock and the pid, and a test is why: two mints inside one second
       from one process gave the SAME id, so a restart landing in a single second would have read as
       the same numbering and the stale lineages would have been trusted. 16 bits of clock in front
       so the ids read in order in a log, 24 random bits after them doing the actual work - 5000
       mints, no collisions. A state file from before this version has no runId, so the freshly
       minted one stands and the plugin drops its stored lineages once, on the upgrade; the counter
       itself is still restored, so nothing else is thrown away.
       The rPi losing its state file at a restart is STILL the underlying fault and still open. This
       is what stops it reaching the indigo devices, and unlike .194.1 it holds for every repeat.

v2022.194.3  @ 2026-09-27
   FIND MY: a down member device is REUSED AS SOON AS IT IS AVAILABLE instead of a new one being
       made, with a new group setting "reuse a down member device after" (default 15 minutes) saying
       when available begins. .194.2 said it handed devices over and it did - but only once every
       unused slot NUMBER had been spent, because the ranking still put an empty slot first as the
       least disruptive thing to take. That is true in itself and completely wrong in effect: each
       empty number spent is a brand new indigo device. MEASURED between two dumps of the same
       group: eight member devices at 18:23 had become EIGHTEEN by 21:35, created straight up the
       empty numbers - 01, 07, 08, 09, 10, 11, 12, 13, 14, 15, 18, then 24 at 21:31 and 25 at 21:34 -
       while THIRTEEN devices sat down for two and three hours untouched, with the down-delay at 90
       seconds so nothing was protecting them. It only ever grows that way, up to maxSlots, and then
       starts reusing anyway.
       FIVE TIERS NOW, cheapest first: a device nobody renamed that is past the reuse time; then an
       unused slot number, which means a new device; then a device nobody renamed that is NOT past it
       yet, because the only thing lost there is the chance its tag was coming back; then a renamed
       one past the time; then a renamed one that is not. Inside each tier the one unheard longest
       goes first, so a three hour headstone is taken before a twenty minute one, and where there is
       a choice the one last closest to the same rPi. Replayed against the 21:35 dump: all thirteen
       headstones are now taken before any new device is made, the old ordering reproduces the
       eighteen devices exactly, a device unheard four minutes is still passed over in favour of an
       empty number, and a renamed device is still the very last thing touched.
       NOT THE SAME FENCE AS "keep a member up for": that one decides how long the device goes on
       showing the tag after it stops being heard, and a slot inside its down-delay is not available
       at all. The reuse time is meant to be the longer of the two. Under about 10 minutes the slot
       of a tag that is merely asleep gets handed on - airpods in a closed case return on the same
       mac every 3 to 4 minutes and the rPi holds a slot for its own mac for 5 minutes besides.

v2022.194.2  @ 2026-09-27
   FIND MY: a member device is never deleted to make room any more - it is HANDED OVER. Deleting an
       indigo device is not ordinary business: it takes the name, the id and everything anybody
       built on it, and it cannot be undone. The reasoning this replaces had it backwards - it said
       a device silently describing a different tag was worse than a deletion because triggers,
       control pages and plots go on pointing at it, and they do, but DELETING IT BREAKS THOSE SAME
       REFERENCES OUTRIGHT, which is strictly worse than having them point at a device that is
       still there and still says what it is. A member device is addressed by its SLOT, so the next
       tag simply takes over the device it finds; the only thing that had to happen was handing back
       a name the user chose, and that now happens at the moment the slot actually changes hands,
       with a log line naming it. The machinery for the rest already existed: newTag resets the
       accumulated apple types instead of merging them when the old and new lineages do not overlap,
       so the kind is relearned and the auto-rename follows - a device moves between kinds freely,
       an airpods case's device reused by an airtag becomes findmy_NN and an airtag's device reused
       by a case becomes findmy_NN_airpod-case.
   FIND MY: a free slot is now taken in the room it came from where there is a choice - one whose
       device was last closest to the same rPi as the incoming tag. AN INDIVIDUAL FIND MY TAG CANNOT
       BE IDENTIFIED FROM ITS FRAMES AT ALL, so what a member device really carries is "a tag near
       this rPi", and a device handed to tags in the same part of the house goes on meaning that
       while one that jumps between rPis means nothing. A device that goes down keeps its closestRPI,
       so it still answers which part of the house it was. It is a TIEBREAK INSIDE a tier and never a
       tier of its own: an empty slot still beats reusing any device and a device nobody renamed
       still beats one somebody named, whatever the rPis say.
   FIND MY: "delete member devices" is now the once-a-day tidy it should always have been, default
       "at 4am". It is no longer how a slot is freed - nothing needs it for that - so all it does is
       stop the list growing findmy_NN headstones for ever. THREE THINGS must all be true: the
       device is still under a name this plugin gave it, it has been down long enough, and NOTHING
       about it changed in that time - not lastSeen, not the mac, not the tagId. The last of those
       is new and matters: a device can be "down" while its slot was handed over an hour ago or a
       rotation was followed through it minutes ago, and anything that moved recently is in use
       whatever its status says. A name the user chose is never deleted by a timer at any age; it is
       the one thing here that cannot be put back, and a device only reaches the tidy unclaimed the
       honest way, by its slot changing hands. 4 hours at 4am means it went before midnight. The
       short minute settings are kept but are now guarded by the same three tests.
       The brief "reuse:15m" setting of .194.1 is gone: it took a chosen name back on a TIMER, which
       moved the device from "somebody named this" to "nobody named this" and so made it PREFERRED
       for reuse - the opposite of what it was for. A value left over in that field deletes nothing.

v2022.194.1  @ 2026-09-27
   FIND MY: a lineage from an rPi whose tagId counter has been REUSED is now thrown away instead of
       matched. The whole value of a tagId is that it is never reused - that is what lets this side
       follow a tag through a mac rotation it cannot see - but the rPi mints them from a counter in
       its state file, and when that file is lost the counter restarts at 1 and hands out ids that
       indigo devices here still remember as OTHER tags. The lineage match is a string compare, so
       from then on it is not following anything, it is pairing strangers that share a number, and
       each wrong pairing drags a device's name and its accumulated apple types onto a tag they
       never belonged to. MEASURED, this afternoon: previousTagIds of 2-20, 2-22, 2-27 and 2-29
       sitting beside current ones of 2-1..2-9 - the counter had restarted at least twice in one
       afternoon - tagId 2-2 held by two devices and 2-4 by two more, one mac on two slots at once,
       and slots 02, 05 and 22 trading macs in a RING, which carried an airpods case's label and
       its name onto a plain tag that had never sent anything but find my. The test needs no stored
       history, which is the point: while all is well the counter is strictly above every id it has
       issued, so a device holding id T while its rPi reports nextTagId <= T is proof on its own
       that the counter went back. A legitimate restart cannot trip it, because the rPi raises the
       counter above everything it restores. Only the offending rPi's entries are dropped - a
       device carrying "2-3,5-7" keeps its rPi-5 half - and the walk runs only on the first message
       after a plugin start or when the counter is seen to fall, never on the ordinary pass.
       NOT A REPAIR, deliberately: the lineage really is unknowable by then, and not matching costs
       one extra device for that tag once, at its next rotation. Matching a reused id corrupts
       identity silently and compounds, which is what the measurement above is.
       The rPi losing its state file at a restart is the underlying fault and is still open; this
       stops it spreading to the indigo devices in the meantime.

   FIND MY: the rPi now knows when IT stopped listening, which it never did before. A beaconloop
       restart, or the handoff from it stalling, is a hole in the frame stream - and a mac that
       rotated during one used to find cands and goneTags both EMPTY (a restart wipes them and

       fresh lineage was minted on the spot with no ROT~ and no 20 s confirmation at all. The log
       line said "none in range at all", which was true and completely misleading: nothing was in
       range because nothing was remembered. The gap is now measured on the way back IN - from
       tick() an emptied room and a stalled feed are identical, so a test there would either trust
       a dead radio for ever or expire a live tag anyway - and a newcomer that cannot be accounted
       for is PARKED for 60 s on the same rotPending mechanism a claim uses, then decided once the

       back with 62 packets in ONE row - the radio had heard it throughout, the handoff had
       withheld it for 52 s - while it sat in another mac's candidate list as "gone".
   FIND MY: a restored lineage no longer lives in the live map. tagIdOf is written at join and
       removed by the expiry pass, which walks the candidates - so an entry restored from a state
       file for a mac that never came back was pruned by nothing and was immortal. Worse than the
       bytes, it was unbounded IN TIME: a state file written a fortnight ago still handed its macs
       their lineages silently, past the two days ownTag deliberately allows and without the ROT=
       line that says it happened. Restored lineages now go into ownTag, dated from the state
       file's own mtime - read BEFORE the reboot restore-copy, whose mtime would claim every mac in
       it was a member a moment ago - so they get the keep policy, the ceiling and the log line
       that already existed, and tagIdOf means what it says: a mac that is here.
   FIND MY: member devices are REUSED now, not deleted, and that is the new default. A member
       device is addressed by its SLOT and never by a tag, so the next tag in a slot gets that
       device whatever it is called - which means renaming it back to its birth name IS the whole
       of freeing it, and there was never anything a deletion achieved except losing the device and
       every trigger, control page and plot pointing at it. "delete member devices" is now "member
       devices when the tag goes", with reuse after 15 minutes down as the default and after an
       hour as the alternative; the durations that DELETE are still there and now say so in the
       menu. The old default was 30m - deleting - while the option list and the help text both
       said "never" was the default, so every group device made since .193 was quietly deleting
       members down half an hour. 15 minutes is chosen against measured behaviour: airpods in a
       closed case return on the SAME mac every 3 to 4 minutes and the rPi holds a slot for its
       mac for 5 minutes on top of that, so anything shorter takes the name off a device whose tag
       is merely asleep.
   FIND MY: a free slot is now preferred in the room it came from. AN INDIVIDUAL FIND MY TAG

       tag near this rPi" - and a device handed to tags in the same part of the house goes on

       its closestRPI (closestRPILast is written on a MOVE, never on the way down), so it still
       answers which part of the house it was, and the rPis hearing the incoming mac come from its
       tagIds. It is a TIEBREAK INSIDE a tier and not a tier of its own: an empty slot still beats
       reusing any device and an unclaimed one still beats a device somebody named, whatever the
       rPis say - the room only ever decides between slots that were already equally cheap.
   FIND MY: the group device dialog is split. Everything an ordinary setup needs - which rPis
       count, the join and leave levels, how many member devices, how long one stays up, what
       happens when its tag goes, one device per member, print members - is above two separator

       statistic and packet counts, debug and calibration are below it. Nothing was renamed or

   FIND MY: rotation matching rebuilt around what the old mac does AFTER it disappears rather than

IN SHORT
   - FIND MY: a new counter for apple find my tags, from imported package to a working feature -
     a group device per house, a device per tag, several rPis merged by mac, distance in metres,
     and a calibration log. The bulk of this entry.
   - THE BLE5 LISTENER was forwarding ble4 advertisements that the ble4 radio had already heard,
     at a level about 40 dB adrift. That polluted EVERY rssi-derived number on a multi-radio rPi,
     not just find my - beacon distance, room assignment, presence thresholds all get better.
   - GREE AND TOSHIBA IR-AC: the temperature could not be changed at all once the unit was in a
     mode indigo has no name for.
   - a "print this device's properties and states" checkbox in all 151 device dialogs.
   - after a plugin restart or a program push to an rPi, nothing goes up-->down for 120 s.

================================================================================
FIND MY - WHAT IT COUNTS
================================================================================
   NEW: a counter for apple Find My tags - airtags and the like - that are in the house.
       A find my tag ROTATES ITS BLE MAC, every ~15 min near its owner and daily when separated,
       so it cannot be followed as a beacon. Every find my mac is a CANDIDATE; a candidate whose
       smoothed signal is above joinRssi, with enough packets behind it, is a MEMBER; and the
       count only moves after the new value has HELD for stableSecs - so a rotation, the old mac
       expiring as the new one appears, never reaches indigo as a change.
       pi side:  findMyGroup.py plus 4 hooks in beaconloop.py (import, setParams in readParams, a
                 frame filter, a tick in the scan loop). All of it no-ops when the module or the
                 parameters block is absent, so an rPi that does not get the file is unaffected.
       tools:    findmy_scan.py on the rPi and findmy_merge.py on the mac, for choosing the rssi
                 thresholds. Both are PYTHON3 ONLY, unlike the rest of pi/*.py - safe because
                 beaconloop never imports them, and each says so in its header.
   CHG: the frame prefilter is "FF4C00" - ANY apple frame - and is no longer narrowed to
       "FF4C0012". A REAL HOUSE DISPROVED THE NARROW FORM: an airpods case sent types 07 and 12 in
       the SAME frame, so find my is not always the first continuity message and "FF4C0012" would
       have dropped that device without trace.

================================================================================
FIND MY - THE DEVICES
================================================================================
   NEW: TWO device types. ONE GROUP DEVICE for the house, and ONE DEVICE PER MEMBER - not 60
       states on one device, and not one device per tag per rPi.
       BLEfindMyGroup carries the summary only: memberCount (settled), memberCountRaw
       (instantaneous), previousCount, lastCountChange, nearbyCount, candidates, strongestRSSI,
       pisReporting, seenBy2Plus, findMyTrigger, status, created.
       BLEfindMyMember is created on demand, "findmy_NN", INTO THE GROUP DEVICE'S OWN FOLDER, and
       is never deleted automatically. RENAME IT - the device name is the label, and nothing the
       plugin writes touches it, which is what twenty name fields in a dialog were standing in for.
       Lookup is by PROPS (findMyGroupDevId + findMySlot), never by name.
   WHAT A MEMBER DEVICE HOLDS: mac, previousMacs, lastMacChange, lastStatusChange, rssi, distance,
       distanceIndicator, battery, appleTypes, slot, status, created - and, new below, one
       signal / distance / time trio per rPi plus closestRPI and closestRPILast.
   NEW: DISTANCE IN METRES. A field in the member's dialog, "signal of THIS tag at 1 metre (dBm)",
       default -70, and state "distance" = calcDist(rssiAt1m, rssi) / distanceUnits - the same
       formula and the same units preference every beacon in this plugin uses.
       The reference is per DEVICE, not per group: every tag model transmits at a different level
       and each dongle has its own offset, so one number for the house would be wrong for most of
       them. The dialog says how to measure it and warns that below a metre the signal is flat -
       3 cm and 1 m read the same - so the result is near / mid / far, not a measurement.
   NEW: PER-RPI SIGNAL, DISTANCE AND TIME on a member device, one trio per rPi, with the same
       state names and the same types a beacon carries - Pi_02_Signal, Pi_02_Distance,
       Pi_02_Time. Same names so anything written for beacons works here unchanged; same TYPES
       because indigo's sql logger makes one column per state NAME across every device type, and
       that is not a nicety (see the collision below).
       An rPi that is enabled but is NOT hearing this tag reads -999 / 999, which is the useful
       half: "heard by rPi-2 only" is then visible on the device instead of having to be read out
       of the group's debug block. _Time is only written when that rPi really did report, so it
       stands still at the last time it did.
       NOT HEARD IS 999, NEVER 0 - for the per-rPi distances and for "distance" itself, when the
       slot empties, when one rPi stops hearing the tag, and as the value stamped on a new device.
       0 means "lying on top of the rPi", the exact opposite of what is being said, and it is a
       value a very close tag really can produce: calcDist floors at 0.01 m, which rounds to 0.0
       at one decimal. Existing devices are corrected where the state lists are refreshed, and
       ONLY where the SIGNAL half of the pair reads -999 - which was the thing that told a tag on
       the rPi from a tag that is not there, before the floor below made it moot.
   CHG: AND A REAL READING IS FLOORED AT 0.5, so no distance state can be 0 at all and the
       ambiguity is gone rather than worked around. Nothing under the floor was a measurement
       anyway: below about a metre the signal is FLAT - 3 cm and 1 m read the same - so down
       there the formula is only running out of range, and it bottoms out at 0.01 m, which is
       what was rounding to 0.0 in the first place.
       So the three values a distance state can hold now each mean one thing: 0.5 "as close as
       this can tell", 999 "not heard", and a number between them an actual estimate. The member
       dialog says so. calcDist's own out-of-range sentinel (99999) is mapped to 999 too, instead
       of being divided by the distance units into a different meaningless number.
   NEW: closestRPI / closestRPIText and closestRPILast / closestRPITextLast, as on a beacon.
       -1 means heard by nobody, which is what a slot that has gone down reads. The Text pair
       carries the rPi's indigo device NAME, because "2" is not something to read off a control
       page.
       GOING DOWN DOES NOT WRITE THE Last PAIR - the same rule the BLEconnect path uses, and this
       is why it is that rule: if -1 displaced the real rPi into closestRPILast, a tag that drops
       out and comes back on the SAME rPi ends up with closestRPI and closestRPILast both naming
       it, which says nothing at all. It was written the other way first and did exactly that.
       Last means "the rPi it was nearest BEFORE this one", so only a move from one REAL rPi to a
       DIFFERENT real one writes it - and with a single rPi it correctly stays -1 for ever.
       Nothing is lost by this: where a tag was last heard is in Pi_NN_Time and in lastSeen.
   NEW: member state "movedRpi" - WHEN the tag last moved from one rPi to another, stamped at
       exactly the moment closestRPI goes from one real rPi to a DIFFERENT real one.
       Deliberately not written for -1 -> rPi: coming back up, or being heard for the first time,
       is not a move, and stamping it there would quietly turn this into "when was it last seen",
       which lastSeen already answers. Same rule as closestRPILast, so the three read together -
       "it was at <closestRPITextLast>, it is at <closestRPIText>, it moved at <movedRpi>".
       And it says so in PLUGIN.LOG at the moment it happens:
         findMyGroup findmy house: Karl keys (mac F2:29:8F:CC:34:8E) moved
         rPi-2 (Pi_kitchen) rssi:-81 -> rPi-5 (Pi_garage) rssi:-68  at 2026-09-23 19:41:07
       BOTH SIGNALS are on the line, because that is what says whether it was a real move or not:
       -81 -> -68 is a tag that went somewhere, -70 -> -69 is two rPis arguing over one that did
       not. A -999 carries the AGE of that rPi's last report with it - "-999 (its last report 2s
       ago)" is an rPi that has really lost the tag, "-999 (its last report 50s ago)" is only a
       report that predates the move, and the two mean opposite things.
   FIX: A TAG DID NOT MOVE JUST BECAUSE THE RPIS REPORT OUT OF STEP. Each rPi sends on its own
       schedule, so a mac missing from one rPi's last message means "it could not hear it WHEN IT
       SENT", not "it cannot hear it" - and closestRPI was handed to whichever rPi happened to
       report most recently, on one-sided information.
       MEASURED, on a stationary tag sitting near the boundary between two rPis:
         00:06:02  moved rPi-2 rssi:-999 -> rPi-5 rssi:-60
         00:06:11  moved rPi-5 rssi:-999 -> rPi-2 rssi:-72
         00:06:12  moved rPi-2 rssi:-72  -> rPi-5 rssi:-60
         00:06:18  moved rPi-5 rssi:-999 -> rPi-2 rssi:-72
       Four moves in sixteen seconds for a tag nobody touched, three of the four decided with the
       other side at -999 purely because its report predated the moment - and it finished on the
       wrong rPi. Nothing in the Find My path damped this; every single message could flip it,
       unlike beacons, where findClosestRPI has always kept the current rPi unless the new one is
       clearly better.
       A challenger now has to STAY strongest for "a move to another rPi counts after:" (new on
       the group device, default 20 s). Flapping never reaches it, because the candidate keeps
       changing and the timer keeps restarting. Replayed against the four lines above: 4 moves
       become 0 and it stays where it belongs; a genuine move with the new rPi holding is
       committed once, 20 s late.
       ONLY rPi -> rPi is damped. Being heard for the first time, and losing the tag altogether,
       carry no such doubt and are taken as they come.
       A dB margin alone would NOT have worked here and that is worth recording: when one side
       reads -999 every comparison passes any margin. The doubt is about the age of the evidence,
       not its size, so the fix has to be in time.
       Level 10, not 20: indigo_log_handler sits at INFO, so 20 would copy every one of these
       into the indigo event log, and a tag sitting near the boundary between two rPis can hand
       back and forth all evening.
       Devices already carrying the duplicate are corrected when their group's state lists are
       refreshed, ie at plugin start and on every save of the group - the two being equal is
       impossible under the rule above, so resetting it is always right.
   THE STATES ARE CREATED DYNAMICALLY, for the rPis actually in use. A member device has no rPi of
       its own - it is a tag, and which rPis can hear it is a property of its GROUP - so its
       per-rPi states follow the GROUP's rPi checkboxes. Indigo rebuilds a device's state list
       only when that device is told to, and it is never told that somebody else's dialog was
       saved, so the rebuild is triggered in two places:
         - after the group device is SAVED, as a delayed action. Delayed for the same reason the
           property print is: indigo writes valuesDict back to the device only after validate
           returns, and a rebuild reading the OLD flags would leave exactly the states just changed
         - at PLUGIN START, in initConcurrentThread. deviceStartComm already rebuilds every state
           list at "init", but it runs before self.RPI is necessarily loaded, and a member built
           from an empty rPi table would carry no per-rPi states until the group was next saved
   FIX: and the reason that had to be built rather than just used - THE PER-RPI FILTER IN
       getDeviceStateList HAS NEVER RUN, for any device type. It tested stateBaseName, which is the
       CATEGORY name ("rpiAndBeaconAndBLEconnect"), while the Pi_NN_ names are the states INSIDE
       that category - so nothing ever started with "Pi_" and the test was never true.
       CONSEQUENCE, AND IT REACHES BEYOND FIND MY: every beacon, rPi and BLEconnect device has
       been carrying all 20 rPis' Signal/Distance/Time states whether those rPis exist or not -
       60 states each, most of them permanently empty. Testing addToState instead makes the filter
       do what its docstring has always claimed, so a switched-off rPi's trio now goes.
       SAFETY: an EMPTY rPi table means the config has not been read yet, NOT that every rPi is
       off - indigo can ask for a state list before readConfig has run. Filtering on that would
       strip all 60 states off every beacon in the house, so when nothing is known, nothing is
       removed.
   NEW: six group states saying WHAT the members are, not just how many - three kinds, each
       split up from down:
         numberOfAirtagsUp / numberOfAirtagsDown   the mac sends find my and NOTHING else
         numberOfAirpodsUp / numberOfAirpodsDown   the 07+12 pair a case sends with its lid open
         numberOfOtherUp   / numberOfOtherDown     anything else, ie a mac that also does
                                                   live-apple-device things
       Counted over the MEMBER DEVICES, each from its own appleTypes. THE THREE ...Up ADD UP TO
       numberOfUp AND ALL SIX ADD UP TO numberOfMembers, and neither is asserted: they are
       tallied at the same three points in the member loop that decide up or down, so they are
       made of that decision and cannot drift from it.
       "airtags" is the short name, not a claim: find-my-only is an airtag or any other find my
       accessory, and nothing in the frame narrows it further - findMyAppleTypes() says why.
       Read from the CODES at the front of appleTypes, never the words after them: the words are
       for a person and get reworded, the codes are the data.
   NEW: optional housekeeping - "delete member devices:" on the group device, off by default,
       with two shapes that answer different questions:
         at 4pm, if down 4 hours or more   a once-a-day tidy. 4 hours down at 4pm means it went
                                           before noon, so it has been missing for most of the
                                           day rather than being out at the shops - the hour and
                                           the threshold belong together, which is why they are
                                           one option and not two knobs
         any time, once down 1..6 hours    acts as soon as the hours are up
       Checked every 5 minutes from the message path rather than on an hourly pass, so "once down
       1 hour" means about that and not "some time in the next hour". Rate limited because it
       walks every indigo device.
       THE DEVICE IS DELETED, NOT EMPTIED, and the dialog says so: the name you gave it goes with
       it, along with anything pointing at it from a trigger, a control page or a plot. A tag that
       comes back gets a fresh findmy_NN, because nothing in a find my frame can tell the plugin
       it is the same tag that used to have a name. That is why the default is never.
       Two guards worth stating: only members found BY PROPS on that group device can be touched,
       and a member that has never been dated is never deleted - lastStatusChange is the only
       evidence of how long it has been down, and "no evidence" must not read as "long enough".
       Every deletion is logged at 20 with the device name, slot, last mac and how long it had
       been down. A device vanishing from the list without a line saying why would be alarming.
   NEW: A DELAY BEFORE A MEMBER GOES DOWN - the same idea as a beacon's expirationTime, and for
       the same reason: a tag advertises every ~2 s, so a handful of lost packets is normal and
       the first report that does not mention it is not evidence that it has gone.
       ONE SETTING ON THE GROUP, "a member stays up for:", used by every member - like the
       join/leave levels. A tag has no dialog of its own worth keeping twenty copies of this in.
       Default 60 s; "no delay" restores the old behaviour.
       A held device is left EXACTLY as it was when last heard - states, uiValue and image -
       rather than half blanked. "up, -68 dBm, 09-23 18:04:11" is the honest picture of a tag
       that has missed a few packets; "up" with an empty signal is not.
       AND ITS SLOT IS NOT GIVEN AWAY while it is held, which is the half that makes it mean
       anything: handing the slot to a new mac would overwrite the device that is deliberately
       still showing the old tag. The mac coming back takes its own slot again, because the slot
       is decided from the device's own "mac" state and the hold does not blank that.
       New member state "lastSeen" - when any rPi last reported this tag, which is what the delay
       is measured against. A STATE and not a dict in memory, so a plugin restart does not hand
       every member a fresh hold and keep long-gone tags showing as up. Existing devices have no
       lastSeen until their state list is rebuilt at the next plugin start, and are simply not
       held until then.
       NOTE WHAT THIS DOES NOT TOUCH: the tag COUNT. That is settled on the Pi and taken as it
       comes, deliberately - settling it twice is what caused "3 tags but 2 up and 2 down". So a
       delay much longer than "count must hold for" means the counter can drop before the member
       devices do. The dialog says so.
   CHG: A MEMBER GOING DOWN NOW BLANKS NOTHING AT ALL - not the mac, battery or apple types, and
       as of this version not the signal, distance or indicator either.
       The earlier reasoning for still clearing those two - "they measure a signal that is no
       longer arriving, so keeping them would be a lie" - does not survive contact with the
       question actually asked when a tag goes: where was it, and how strong, when it went. A
       last measurement with a time beside it is a RECORD. A row of -999 and 999 is not, and it
       destroys the only copy of the answer.
       ONE STATE SAYS UP OR DOWN, and that is status (with onOffState). Overwriting six
       measurement states to repeat what status already says was never worth what it cost.
       The same goes per rPi: an rPi that is not hearing the tag right now leaves its trio alone
       instead of stamping -999/999 over it. Pi_NN_Time says when each reading was taken, which
       distinguishes a current reading from an old one far better than every silent rPi reporting
       the same sentinel.
       SO -999 AND 999 NOW MEAN EXACTLY ONE THING for the life of a device: that rPi has NEVER
       heard this tag. They are stamped once when the device is made - signals included now, they
       were defaulting to 0, ie full strength - and never written again.
   NEW: previousTagIds and lastTagIdChange on a member device - the lineage history, for the same
       reason previousMacs exists for the mac. A LINEAGE CHANGE WITH NO MAC CHANGE is the
       signature of a WRONG rotation match: the rPi gave this mac's lineage to a newcomer while
       this mac was still transmitting. The repair overwrites the evidence a packet or two later,
       so it could only be seen by watching live, which is not a way to debug anything.
       Read with lastMacChange beside it, the pair says WHICH of the two moved - and that is the
       whole question when two devices end up holding one lineage.
   NEW: previousStatusChange on a member device - the up/down change BEFORE the last one.
       A member that dips down and is back a few seconds later overwrote lastStatusChange on the
       way back, so the dip left no trace at all unless somebody happened to be watching the log
       as it happened. With both stamps it is stated rather than inferred: "previous 19:52:48,
       last 19:52:51" is three seconds down, and "previous 19:52:51, last 20:05:12" is twelve
       minutes up. Written ONLY on a real change, and only when there was something to inherit,
       so it never becomes a second copy of the line beside it. It sits next to lastStatusChange
       in the members table for the same reason.
   NEW: Pi_NN_State, up / down per rPi - whether THAT rPi is hearing the tag right now.
       This is what makes the above safe rather than ambiguous. The other three per-rPi states
       hold the last measurement and are never blanked, so something has to say whether they are
       live or historical, and a sentinel written over the reading was the wrong way to say it:
       it answered "is it here" by destroying "where was it". Now the readings are the readings
       and Pi_NN_State is the answer.
       Reading a member device is then: Pi_02_State up, Pi_02_Signal -65, Pi_02_Time 00:57:44 -
       heard by rPi-2 now, at -65. Or Pi_02_State down with the same signal and time - that is
       what rPi-2 last heard, and when, and it is not hearing it now.
   CHG: and it has FOUR values, not two, because "down" was covering three different situations
       and two of them were misleading:
         up      in that rPi's member list - the trio beside it is live
         weak    it HEARS the tag and does not count it: below joinRssi, or short of minPackets.
                 That is a threshold to adjust, not a tag that has gone, and the two were
                 indistinguishable on this side because the rPi only ever sent its MEMBERS
         noData  that rPi is not reporting at all. It said nothing about this tag because it is
                 saying nothing about anything - reading "down" for an rPi that is unplugged
                 claimed it could not hear the tag, which was simply wrong
         down    reporting, and this tag is in nothing it sent
       A trigger for "not properly here" is != "up", which catches all three.
       "weak" NEEDED THE RPI TO SEND MORE: it now carries every mac it hears but does not count,
       mac -> rssi, capped at MAXWEAK (40) strongest first - they have no slot and never will, so
       they cannot travel in "members". An rPi on an older findMyGroup.py sends none and then
       nothing is ever weak, which is exactly what this side knew before.
       "noData" NEEDED NOTHING NEW: the plugin already knows which rPis are reporting, it just
       was not saying so. The weak lookup uses the DEVICE'S OWN mac when the tag is not in the
       union, which is the case that matters most - the tag has stopped counting, and whether an
       rPi can still faintly hear it is exactly what one wants to know then.
   CHG: closestRPI is no longer blanked either. It used to go to -1 the moment nobody could hear
       the tag, which threw away "where was it last seen" in order to say something the
       Pi_NN_State states and status now say properly. It holds the rPi that heard it LAST.
       A side effect worth having: a tag that disappears and comes back on a DIFFERENT rPi is now
       a real rPi-to-rPi move, so it is logged and stamped in movedRpi. Going through -1 hid
       exactly those - the two halves each looked like "first acquisition", which is not a move.
       -1 still has a meaning, just a narrower one: no rPi has EVER heard this device.
   CHG: THE GROUP'S COUNTS ARE ONE FAMILY OF NAMES, ON BOTH SIDES. They had grown into three
       vocabularies at once - memberCount, membersUp, nearbyCount, candidates, numberTotal - and
       "memberCount versus membersUp" told nobody which was which. Every count is numberOf* now:
         numberOfMembers          member devices that exist        (was membersTotal)
         numberOfUp               of those, currently up           (was membersUp)
         numberOfActive           macs in the LAST message, settled (was memberCount)
         numberOfActiveRaw        the same, instantaneous          (was memberCountRaw)
         numberOfActivePrevious   what active was before it moved  (was previousCount)
         numberOfActiveChanged    when it last moved               (was lastCountChange)
         numberOfWeak             heard and NOT counted            (was nearbyCount)
         numberOfCandidates       every find my mac heard          (was candidates)
         numberOfAirtagsUp / numberOfAirtagsDown / numberOfAirpodsUp / numberOfAirpodsDown
         numberOfOtherUp / numberOfOtherDown / numberOfTotal
       ACTIVE is transmitting right now; UP is present, which includes a tag inside
       memberDownDelaySecs that simply was not in the last report. That pair is the distinction
       that kept being asked about, and the words now carry it instead of a comment.
       numberOfWeak IS ALSO A CORRECTION, not just a rename: "nearbyCount" never meant nearby. It
       is len(active) - len(members), the macs an rpi hears and does not count, and it sat next to
       distanceIndicator's "nearby" meaning something entirely different. It now matches the
       "weak" value Pi_NN_State uses for the same thing on a member device.
       THE WIRE FORMAT CHANGED WITH IT. The rpi's message fields carried the old names while the
       states carried new ones, which is two vocabularies for one number in a different place.
       Both sides renamed together, so EVERY RPI MUST BE PUSHED with this version - an rpi still
       sending the old fields is not understood at all, which is deliberate: a loud mismatch is
       better than a silent half-rename.
       An existing group device loses the old states when its state list is rebuilt, so anything
       pointed at memberCount, nearbyCount or candidates needs repointing.
   NEW: membersUp on the group device, AND IT IS WHAT THE STATE COLUMN NOW SHOWS. The device kept
       raising a question it could not answer: "7 members" beside thirteen member devices reading
       up, both numbers right and the pair looking like neither was.
       THEY ANSWER "PRESENT" WITH DIFFERENT EVIDENCE. memberCount is the macs in the LAST MESSAGE.
       A member device stays up for memberDownDelaySecs after it stops being heard, because one
       report that does not mention a tag is not evidence the tag has gone. With a 90 s delay and
       a 60 s send interval, six tags heard 31 to 54 s ago are up and are not in the count - so
       the two could never agree, and explaining that every time is not a design.
       The number on the group now answers the same question its devices answer. numberOfActive
       and numberOfActiveRaw are untouched and still mean exactly what they meant.
       THE COLUMN READS "13/20 up" - up out of how many member devices EXIST. numberOfMembers is
       the second number and is a state of its own: it is the one people go and count by hand in
       the device list, and it is what says whether slots are filling with tags that have gone. On
       one screen: how many are here, how many devices there are, and the gap between them.
       AND THE GROUP NO LONGER CLAIMS A SENSOR VALUE AT ALL - it is a CUSTOM device type now, and
       those two go together. "13/20 up" is text, and sensorValue is indigo's NUMERIC state:
       handing it that as a uiValue used a number field as a text field with a meaningless number
       hidden behind it, and made the device's one real number a duplicate of a count that already
       had a state of its own.
       DROPPING THE CLAIM WAS ONLY HALF OF IT, and the half that did nothing on its own. A device
       supporting a sensor value has its state column FORCED to sensorValue, exactly as an on/off
       device has its forced to onOffState - but a SENSOR supporting neither draws NOTHING AT ALL.
       UiDisplayStateId is a CUSTOM device type's feature and a sensor device never looks at it.
       Measured, not reasoned: with SupportsSensorValue false the server reported displayStateId
       "status", status held "3/3 up" and status.ui held "3/3 up   09-24 23:52:09", and the column
       was EMPTY. Hence type="custom", which is the type this device should always have been -
       what it shows is a sentence, and it has no single measurement to be a sensor for.
       NOTHING IS LOST BY IT: the counts are numberOfUp, numberOfMembers, numberOfActive and
       numberOfActiveRaw, every one a real Integer and a better thing to plot or trigger on than
       sensorValue was. Group devices made before this carry the old property and are corrected
       once at startup, with a line in the log saying so - and that correction now tests what
       indigo DRAWS rather than what the property says, so it retries until the column is right
       instead of flipping the property once and trusting the rebuild that follows.
       The stamp beside it is when the shown TEXT last changed, keyed on the text itself rather
       than on either number in it, so it dates exactly the thing next to it whichever half moved
       - a timestamp dating a different number is how this started.
       AND THE DEVICE DUMP NOW PRINTS BOTH: "state column shows 'status' = '3/3 up  09-24 23:52'".
       displayStateId is the state the server PICKED, displayStateValUi is the STRING the client
       puts in the column. This whole evening was the two disagreeing with nothing printing the
       second one, so a blank column now says in one line whose fault it is.
   CHG: the group device says "6 members", not "6 tags". memberCount counts every find my device
       in the house - airtags, an airpods case, a phone or a mac that is advertising - and calling
       that "tags" collided head-on with the airtag counter, which counts only the real ones. On
       a real device: "6 tags" in the state column beside 6 airtags and 1 airpods.
       Three numbers, no two of which agreed. "member" matches the states it belongs with -
       memberCount, memberCountRaw, memberDownDelaySecs - and leaves "tags" meaning tags.
   FIX: A DOWN MEMBER KEEPS ITS KIND AND KEEPS ITS PLACE IN THE BREAKDOWN. The airtag / airpods
       / other counters were taken over the LIVE UNION - the macs heard in the last report - so a
       tag that stopped advertising left the breakdown entirely. Airpods earpieces do exactly
       that, all the time: they go back in the case and go silent. The group then read 0 airpods
       with two member devices sitting in the list both saying airpods, which is not a lag or a
       rounding, it is just wrong.
       COUNTED OVER THE MEMBER DEVICES NOW, each from its own appleTypes - which a member has
