   - "full" on the remote is the turbo bit, not a fan position.
   - only message 5 carries the louvers, even when a louver is what changed.
   - left and right are the VIEWER'S, verified at the unit.

TWO BUGS THAT COST THE MOST TIME, both worth remembering
   - greeIR.CARRIERHZ was left at 30000 from an experiment. Every caller that does not pass a
     frequency used it, receiveCommands included, so the plugin transmitted at 30 kHz while the
     frames stayed perfect. A module default that only SOME callers override.
   - the four IR-AC actions wrote a setting to the server and then sent the state from the local
     device object, which replacePluginPropsOnServer does not update - so the change landed one
     send late. All five paths re-read the device now.

TOOLING (pi/)
   irRecord.py   record and decode a remote, with an echo mode that replays what it heard
   irReplay.py   replay a capture, one message per waveform, keeping the gaps
   irScan.py     walk the carrier 25-48 kHz sending a real command at each step
   irDemo.py     scripted walks - basic, louver, louver2 - to watch the unit follow
   irTest.sh     all of it as single commands, with a 15 s lead-in to reach the other room

v2022.191.301  @ 2026-09-03
   IR: irDemo walk trimmed to the sequence worth watching now - off, on, 20/21/23 C, the five fan
       speeds, off. The louvers are dropped, they already work. It opens at 25 C so that the first
       temperature step is a real change rather than a no-op.

v2022.191.300  @ 2026-09-03
   DOC: the ac BEEPS on every frame it accepts, which is a better oracle than the display and
       splits the two failures that look the same from across the room - "beep but nothing
       changed" is a field question, "no beep" is a reception one. irDemo says so before and after
       the walk, and the README has it as its own section. Half of today would have been shorter
       with that distinction in hand.

v2022.191.299  @ 2026-09-03
   IR: BYTE6FROMFAN back to True - the measured per-fan value is the right one. With the carrier
       fixed the unit obeys power, temperature and the louvers, and its FAN does not move at all
       while byte 6 is a constant, even though byte 0 steps 09/29/39 across the fan settings. So
       byte 0's two bits are not what the unit reads for the fan; byte 6 of message 3 is, exactly
       as the ten press capture showed. The .293 revert was chasing the carrier bug.
   IR: irDemo walks four temperatures now (21, 20, 25, 18, 22) instead of one. "Temperature does
       not change" turned out to be the walk changing it once and then carrying the same value -
       thin test, not a fault.

v2022.191.298  @ 2026-09-03
   IR: THE REGRESSION - greeIR.CARRIERHZ was left at 30000 from the carrier hunt, and every caller
       that does not pass a frequency was transmitting at 30 kHz. That includes receiveCommands,
       so EVERY indigo IR-AC command has gone out at 30 kHz since .269 took the explicit carrier
       out of the send path. The unit answers at 38 kHz. Default is 38000 now, matching toshibaIR.
       This is why "irTest.sh c 38000 6" worked and irDemo did not: c passes the carrier, irDemo
       relied on the default. Identical frames, different carrier.
   NOTE the byte 6 revert in .293 therefore rests on nothing. c ALWAYS passed 38 kHz, so the
       fan-dependent byte 6 was never actually tested at a carrier the unit listens to - the walk
       that failed was failing on the carrier. BYTE6FROMFAN should be set True again and retested
       now that the carrier is right; the measured per-fan value is probably correct.

v2022.191.297  @ 2026-09-03
   IR: irDemo opens by switching the unit OFF, so the next step is a transition that has to show.
       It used to open with "power ON, cool 21, fan auto" - which an ac already in that state
       obeys with no visible change, looking exactly like a step it ignored. The previous
       "irTest.sh c" run leaves the unit on at 21 or 25 C, so this was easy to walk into. That is
       also why c looks conclusive: it alternates 21/25 C, so every send must move the display.
       The banner now says the run takes two minutes and warns about the no-op case.

v2022.191.296  @ 2026-09-03
   IR: irDemo starts with the louvers UNTOUCHED - swingV "last", swingH "off", byte 4 = 00 - so
       its first step is byte for byte the command that is known to work. It had been setting both
       louvers to "middle" (byte 4 = 44), which was the ONLY difference between the walk and that
       command, and the walk got no reaction at all. A unit with no horizontal louver has no use
       for swingH, so the louver steps now move swingV alone and a 12th step puts it back to
       untouched. If the walk still does nothing at step 1, the frames are not the problem and
       irDemo is not running.

v2022.191.295  @ 2026-09-03
   IR: CONFIRMED by testing both ways - the gree unit obeys message 3 carrying the fixed 0xD0 and
       ignores everything when byte 6 follows the fan instead. That is the regression that made
       it go silent, and BYTE6FROMFAN=False is the working setting.
       It is NOT understood. The remote demonstrably sends 0x80 for fan auto - it is in the ten
       press capture and greeIR rebuilds those frames byte for byte - so a unit that obeys its own
       remote should obey 0x80 too. Both observations are solid and they disagree.
       Known cost of the constant: byte 0's fan field saturates at 3, so with byte 6 fixed there
       is nothing left to separate fan 2, 3 and 4 - they emit identical frames. Auto, silent and
       fan 1 still differ, so four distinct fan settings instead of six.

v2022.191.294  @ 2026-09-03
   IR: irDemo.py and irScan.py wait 15 s before their first send, counting down, so they can be
       started over ssh and watched at the ac however they are launched - the wait in irTest.sh
       only helped when the run went through it. IRLEADIN=<seconds> overrides, IRLEADIN=0 skips,
       and irTest.sh sets IRLEADIN=0 after its own countdown so a run through it does not wait
       twice.

v2022.191.293  @ 2026-09-03
   IR: byte 6 of gree message 3 goes back to the fixed 0xD0 - the frame the unit was seen to obey
       when "irTest.sh c 38000 6" worked. The measured, remote-accurate value (BYTE6BASE + the
       fan index) is kept behind BYTE6FROMFAN, which is False for now: two things changed between
       the working test and the silence - this, and the led rig was rebuilt - so they get moved
       one at a time.
       The self test still asserts every other byte of every recorded press; only message 3's
       byte 6 and its checksum are exempted while the flag is False, and it says so.
       NOTE the checksum covers byte 6's high nibble, so it moves with it: the .266 message 3 is
       09 05 20 70 00 00 D0 50, not ...D0 00 as an earlier comparison here had it.

v2022.191.292  @ 2026-09-03
   IR: irTest.sh waits 15 s at the start, counting down, so a run can be kicked off over ssh and
       watched at the ac in the other room. One call at the top, for the modes that transmit -
       not for "a", which already waits for a button press, and not for an unknown argument,
       which should print the usage straight away. LEADIN=<seconds> overrides it, LEADIN=0 starts
       at once.
   IR: greeIR.py - the working copy had been replaced by the 1 Sep file with today's version left
       beside it as "greeIR copy.py". The two were CODE IDENTICAL, 380 executable lines each,
       differing only in the three comment blocks added today, so nothing transmitted ever
       changed. The documented one is active again and the stray copy is gone - it would have
       been pushed to every rpi by the sftp "put *".

v2022.191.291  @ 2026-09-03
   IR: pi/irDemo.py - a scripted walk to WATCH the ac follow. on / cool 21 / 20 C / fan 1,2,3,4 /
       auto / louver up, middle, down / off, ten seconds apart, ONE setting moving per step so a
       step the unit does not follow names itself. Prints all four messages of every press.
       "irTest.sh d" runs it; arguments are gpio, seconds, brand, duty. It goes through greeIR and
       the led, not through indigo, so it tests the encoder, the sequence and the emitter - drive
       the device from indigo and watch the same way to cover the rest.

v2022.191.290  @ 2026-09-03
   IR: cross-checked every gree field against the IRremoteESP8266 spreadsheet. It agrees with all
       of ours and settles the one open question: byte 4 bit 7 is NOT USED - swingH is THREE bits
       (byte4 4-6), not the nibble it looks like - so the bit that came back set on 3 presses of
       10 carries nothing. Sending 0 is right and the variation was never a signal.
       Three more namings taken from it: byte3's high nibble is their "unkown1", the packet
       number, matching our INDEXES; the 3 footer bits are what they call the Marker, so our old
       MARKER constant was misnamed twice over; byte2 bit 6, the one the health button moves on
       this remote, they call ModelA. And byte 6 is unused in the first packet, which is exactly
       why packet 7 is free to carry the real fan position there.
       The whole map is now a comment block above checksum().

v2022.191.289  @ 2026-09-03
   IR: checked the published sources. IRremoteESP8266 issue #1724 - the library ESPHome and
       Tasmota both use - reaches the SAME reading of gree byte 3 from a different remote: it is a
       packet number, 0x5 on the first packet and 0x6 on the second, with later packets carrying
       different content in bytes 4-6. That independently confirms the message-index model here.
       It documents TWO packets and only for the timer button; four packets on every press,
       stepping 5/6/7/A, is not in it or in anything else found. Recorded in greeIR.

v2022.191.288  @ 2026-09-03
   DOC: README gains "Adding a brand" - what can be derived from remote recordings (the encoder,
       with the checksum as the strong check) and what cannot (whether a correct frame is
       SUFFICIENT, the carrier, and fields that saturate and hide their real range). The gree
       needed all three learned at the unit itself.

v2022.191.287  @ 2026-09-03
   gpio: the pin handover no longer logs at all on the normal path. One output told to go up,
       then down, then up hands its own pin over every time and the generation counter steps with
       it - none of which is worth reading, and printing it made routine traffic look like a
       fault. It drops to level 10 when a device hands over to ITSELF, and speaks at level 20 only
       when the new owner is a DIFFERENT indigo device (two devices on one pin, last one wins) or
       an IR-ac send (which ends by driving the pin low, so the output cannot stay on). Those are
       the two cases where an output really refuses to stay on and the reason is otherwise
       invisible.

v2022.191.286  @ 2026-09-03
   gpio: "leaves the output alone" said nothing about what actually happens. The line now names
       the action: the finishing generation does NOT close the pin, because closing releases the
       gpio and the level goes with it - which would drop an output the new owner is holding.
       That is the whole reason the generation counter exists.

v2022.191.285  @ 2026-09-03
   gpio: the pin handover line read like an error and usually is not one. Telling the same output
       to go up while a previous thread still parks on that pin is ordinary traffic - the line
       only records that the older thread left the output alone on its way out, which is what the
       generation counter exists for. It says "handover" now, and the IR-ac caveat added in .281
       is attached ONLY when the new owner really is an IR send, where it matters.

v2022.191.284  @ 2026-09-03
   IR: sendIRacState says so when the device is DISABLED in indigo. The menu and the IR-AC actions
       go through the plugin, not through indigo's device routing, so a disabled IR-AC device
       still transmits and the ac still obeys - while indigo shows it greyed out and its own
       thermostat controls do nothing. That asymmetry was silent.
   NOTE for the record: the IR-AC device needs NO entry in a pi's output table. receiveCommands
       dispatches the IR path on the COMMAND ("irAC"), not on the device type, and takes the pin
       from the command itself - OUTPUTthermostatIRac appears nowhere in receiveCommands.py. So
       an empty entry, or none at all, in the pi parameters is correct and not a fault, and
       moving the led to another gpio needs no cleanup on the pi.

v2022.191.283  @ 2026-09-03
   IR: the "Send an IR-AC command" menu had the same stale-object fault as the four actions - it
       wrote acMode/acFanSpeed to the server and then sent the state from the local device object,
       which still carried the previous values. It re-reads the device before sending now, so all
       five paths that change a setting and resend are consistent.

v2022.191.282  @ 2026-09-03
   IR: the four IR-AC actions wrote the new setting to the server and then sent the state from
       the LOCAL device object, which replacePluginPropsOnServer does not update - so the frame
       went out with the props as they were BEFORE the change and the new fan / mode / louver /
       option only took effect on the NEXT send. Each now re-reads the device before sending.
   gpio: the claim label for setGPIO was the whole command dict, which made the handover line
       unreadable. It is "setGPIO <cmd> dev:<id>" now.

v2022.191.281  @ 2026-09-03
   gpio: the pin handover message now NAMES the successor. "a newer command owns the pin now" is
       correct behaviour - a finished thread must not yank an output that already belongs to
       something else - but it left out the one thing worth knowing at that moment: which command
       took it. Every claim is labelled (setGPIO <cmd>, irAC gree, irAC toshiba, irAC ledTest) and
       the handover line prints the new generation and its label, plus the reminder that an IR-ac
       send ENDS BY DRIVING THE PIN LOW, so an on/off output sharing that pin will not stay on.

v2022.191.280  @ 2026-09-02
   DOC: the IR driver drawing now shows all three connections to the rpi header - +5 V, GPIO 18
       and GND - instead of leaving ground as a floating earth symbol. A fixed-width text version
       of the same circuit sits beside it in the README, for reading in a terminal or an ssh
       session where the svg is no use.

v2022.191.279  @ 2026-09-01
   DOC: the IR led driver as actually built - 560 ohm to the base, 2 ohm ballast, three IR940, a
       green led + 4.7k across the same branch as a "it is sending" indicator, 15k pull-down at
       the gpio line. Works across the room. Drawn in irLedCircuit.svg next to the
       README, which now carries the picture and the numbers: 4.6 mA of base current at 560 ohm,
       ~120 mA through the string at the hFE a small NPN has left there, and at that current the
       IR forward voltage leaves only ~0.7 V of headroom so the 2 ohm takes a third of it.

v2022.191.278  @ 2026-09-01
   DOC: README explains why the 1.8 ohm at the collector earns its place. Three LEDs plus a
       saturated transistor leave only ~1.2 V of headroom on 5 V, so 180 mV at 100 mA is 15 % of
       it and 360 mV at 200 mA is 30 % - real negative feedback against runaway on a steep LED
       curve. Also records that the working point is set by the BASE drive, not by that resistor:
       1.8k from 3.3 V less the 10k pull-down is ~1.37 mA of base current, ~82 mA of collector
       current at hFE 60, which is the 80 mA measured. The transistor runs in its active region.

v2022.191.277  @ 2026-09-01
   DOC: README's air conditioner section brought up to date - the gree fan is five speeds plus
       auto with "full" being the turbo bit, the frame's fan field saturates at 3 so the real
       position rides in a later message, one press is four messages and the unit acts on nothing
       less, only message 5 carries the louvers, and the indigo mappings for fan always-on and
       for auto (heatSetpoint/coolSetpoint sent as their midpoint). New "When an AC ignores
       everything" section with the irTest.sh steps in the order that isolates fastest, and the
       two rules that would have saved a session: put the unit in the opposite state before
       replaying, and never conclude anything from a single success. The pi file list names
       irRecord/irReplay/irScan/irTest.

v2022.191.276  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: indigo's AUTO hvac mode was sending the COOL setpoint and silently dropping the heat one.
       Indigo's auto switches between heating and cooling by comparing the room to the heat
       setpoint and the cool setpoint, which cannot happen here - the IR device is blind, nothing reports back, there
       is no room temperature. The AC's own auto does that job with its own sensor, so the two
       setpoints now collapse to their MIDPOINT, the centre of the band indigo was asked to hold,
       and the log says so whenever the two differ by more than half a degree.

v2022.191.275  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: the "IR-AC: set fan speed" action and the "Send an IR-AC command" menu both build their
       list from _GlobalIRacFans, so they already carry the new gree speeds - silent/1/2/3/4 and
       "full" - and "full" resolves to top speed + turbo whichever path it arrives from. Their
       help text still said "gree 1..3"; corrected, with a note on why speeds above 3 exist at
       all when the frame's fan field only holds two bits.

v2022.191.274  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: indigo's thermostat fan is auto or always-on and nothing else, so "auto" is dropped from
       the gree always-on list - it is the other indigo mode and needs no entry. The list is the
       remote's real speeds silent/1/2/3/4/full, defaulting to 3.

v2022.191.273  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: indigo's thermostat knows only auto and always-on, so the gree "fan speed for always on"
       list is the COARSE one now - auto / 1 / 3 / full - instead of all five speeds. "full"
       means the top speed WITH the turbo bit, which is what the remote's full button actually
       does. The plugin's own IR-AC menu and set-fan action still reach every speed, and carry
       "full" as well.

v2022.191.272  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: the GREE FAN IS FIVE SPEEDS, not three. The remote cycles silent/1/2/3/4/full/auto, and
       byte 0 only has two bits for it - it SATURATES at 3. The real position is the high nibble
       of byte 6 in message 3, which counts 8,9,A,B,C,D right through the cycle while byte 0
       sticks at 3 for the top three speeds. A full ten press cycle shows it, and "full" is not
       a fan position at all: it is the turbo bit, and pressing it left byte 6 where it was.
       FANS is now the remote's own labels {auto, silent, 1, 2, 3, 4}, byte 0 gets min(index,3),
       and buildSequence/sendSequence take the fan because it CANNOT be recovered from a state
       whose byte 0 has already saturated. The whole cycle is in the self test.
       The old 24 recorded states were labelled with our names, which pinned down only the
       saturating field: their 1/2/3 are the remote's silent/1/2, and they are relabelled.
   IR: the device's gree fan list and the action/menu fan lists carry the five speeds.
   NOTE unresolved: on 3 of 10 presses message 3 came back with byte 4 = 0x80 instead of 00, with
       the checksum agreeing either way, and a fourth press at the same fan gave 00. Nothing
       visible decides it, so message 3 is built with byte 4 = 0 - what 7 of the 10 showed. The
       unit does not appear to check it; it obeyed a sequence whose byte 6 was wrong for its fan.

v2022.191.271  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: byte 6 of gree message 3 FOLLOWS THE FAN - it was hardcoded to 0xD0 from a single capture
       and that is only right for fan 3. Five more presses show fan 3 -> D0 across a temperature
       and a turbo change, fan auto -> 80 across a louver change. It is a lookup now, keyed by
       the fan in byte0 bits 4-5; fan 1 and 2 are not measured yet and fall back to the auto
       value. Not a fault the unit notices - it accepted a sequence carrying D0 for fan auto -
       but the frames should match the remote.
   IR: CONFIRMED over those five presses - messages 2 and 3 carry bytes 0-2 of the command and
       nothing else, byte 4 (the louvers) stays in message 1 even when it is the thing that
       changed, and the terminator 00 00 00 A8 00 00 00 20 never varies. All five presses are
       now regression cases in greeIR's self test, which rebuilds every one of their four frames.

v2022.191.270  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: recording a SEQUENCE of presses now lists every message of each press with its index
       nibble, not just message 1. A gree press is four messages and the question a sequence
       recording is usually asked - how the later messages follow the command - was exactly what
       reporting only the first one hid.

v2022.191.269  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: the carrier frequency machinery is removed again - it was built to chase a false lead and
       the answer turned out to be the message sequence. Gone: the per brand carrier fields and
       the CARRIER SWEEP button on the IR-AC device, _GlobalIRacCarriers, the sweep callback and
       its worker, the echo carrier selector in the record menu, and the carrier parameter
       through receiveCommands and irRecord. The duty cycle setting stays, it is a real one.
       pi/irScan.py and irReplay's carrier argument stay as command line diagnostics: they are
       where to start if a future unit really is built for 33 or 36 kHz.

v2022.191.268  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: CONFIRMED - the gree unit obeys greeIR's four message sequence at 38 kHz. Controlled
       comparison, same encoder and same carrier and same harness, only the sequence differing:
           irTest.sh c 38000 6 4   four identical repeats of one frame  -> ignored
           irTest.sh c 38000 6     the four message sequence            -> works
       So it was never the carrier and never the frame contents. A press is four messages -
       state (index 5), two continuations (6, 7) and a terminator carrying no command (A) - and
       this unit acts on nothing less. greeIR is v1.1 and its self test now rebuilds all four
       from the recorded press on every run.
       The earlier replay results were misleading in both directions: m 1..4 replayed a recorded
       power-OFF into an ac that was already off, so a success would have been invisible, and a
       replay carries the receiver's stretched marks (~700 us against the 620 the remote sends)
       which the encoder does not.

v2022.191.267  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: "irTest.sh r <hz> <n>" replays the whole recorded press n times at ONE carrier. b and m
       send the SAME waveform - b just sends it 11 times, once per frequency, where m sends it
       once. m fails at every count and b works, so what b has is ATTEMPTS, not frequency
       diversity, and that points at a marginal optical link rather than at the frame or the
       carrier. r measures it: which attempt lands is the hit rate.
   NOTE that doubt is WITHDRAWN, see .268 - the sequence is the answer.

v2022.191.266  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: greeIR sends the remote's FOUR MESSAGE SEQUENCE. One press is the state (message index 5),
       two continuations carrying the command bytes only (6, 7) and a terminator carrying no
       command at all (A) - told apart by byte3's high nibble, which is a message index and not
       the fixed MARKER the name suggested. Every ground truth recording read 0x50 only because
       every one of them was a first message.
       Measured, and all four verify against checksum():
           31 04 20 50 44 C0 00 F0 / 31 04 20 60 00 00 00 F0 /
           31 04 20 70 00 00 D0 C0 / 00 00 00 A8 00 00 00 20
       buildSequence() regenerates all four byte for byte from the state alone.
       Ruled out first: one frame is ignored at every carrier from 26 to 40 kHz, four identical
       repeats of it are ignored, and messages 1+2 alone are ignored. Only the whole press works.
   IR: receiveCommands sends via sendSequence, and the log names all four messages.
       "irTest.sh c <hz> <n>" now sends the sequence; pass a repeat count to get the old single
       frame behaviour back for comparison.

v2022.191.265  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: the carrier was NOT the problem. A faithful replay of the remote's whole press drives the
       gree unit anywhere from 26 to 40 kHz, 38 included, while a single greeIR frame drives it
       at none of them. So the GREE carrier default goes back to 38000 - the 26 kHz reading in
       .264 was two variables moving at once and is withdrawn. The field stays, because receivers
       really are built for one frequency and a unit needing 33 or 36 cannot be reached at 38.
   IR: what the unit was ignoring is the SEQUENCE - the remote sends four messages, we send one
       frame twice. Two new discriminators: "irTest.sh c <hz> <n> <repeats>" sends one frame with
       a settable repeat count (is it just more repeats?), and "irTest.sh m <hz> <count>" replays
       only the first <count> RECORDED messages, so the sequence can be bisected with the real
       frames and the encoder out of the picture.

v2022.191.264  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: the gree unit reacts, twice in a row, to a faithful replay of its remote's whole press at
       26 kHz - where 38 kHz never worked once in a whole session. GREE carrier default is now
       26000. Two things changed together though: the frequency AND sending all four messages
       instead of our single frame twice, so which one mattered is not yet known.
       "irTest.sh c 26000 10" separates them: it sends ONE greeIR frame at that carrier.

v2022.191.263  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: the gree remote sends FOUR messages per press and they are NOT identical. Two captures of
       the same button, one strong and one deliberately weak, decode byte for byte the same:
       31 04 20 50 44 C0 00 F0 / 31 04 20 60 00 00 00 F0 / 31 04 20 70 00 00 D0 C0 /
       00 00 00 A8 00 00 00 20 - byte3's high nibble stepping 5,6,7,A. Reception errors do not
       reproduce across signal levels, and all four messages are structurally perfect at 139
       pulses each, so this is data. We have only ever sent message 1, twice.
   IR: irReplay v1.3 replays a capture MESSAGE BY MESSAGE - splitting at the headers, one message
       per wave, sleeping the gap the remote left after each. Until now a full press could not be
       replayed at all: the first version split at a pulse count and put socket round trips
       inside frames, and the fix after that reduced it to message 1. So "send exactly what the
       remote sent" has never actually been tested.
   IR: irTest.sh a saves the whole capture again, b replays all of it.

v2022.191.262  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: irTest.sh tests irScan.py with -s, not -f. A file can arrive EMPTY: sftp "put *" copies
       whatever is on disk at that instant, so a push that lands while a file is being written
       leaves a 0 byte copy - and python3 runs an empty file happily, exits 0 and prints nothing,
       which is indistinguishable from a command that did nothing.

v2022.191.261  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: irTest.sh prints its usage for an unknown or missing argument instead of exiting silently,
       and says so when irScan.py is not on the rpi yet. Without that, an OLD copy of the script
       on a pi that has not had the files pushed looks exactly like a command that ran and did
       nothing.

v2022.191.260  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: pi/irScan.py - walks the carrier in 1 kHz steps, 25..48 kHz by default, sending a real
       command at each one 5 s apart. The temperature ALTERNATES 19/21 C, so every send that
       reaches the unit changes its display and a working frequency cannot hide behind the state
       already being right. Goes through greeIR/toshibaIR, never through a replay, so one message
       is always one wave - the biggest is 5075 pulses at 48 kHz against the 5400 limit.
       "irTest.sh s" runs it; arguments are first/last kHz, step, seconds, gpio, brand, duty.

v2022.191.259  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: irReplay v1.2 no longer splits a transmission across waves - it refuses. A capture holds
       2-4 repeats, which is 12000-22000 pigpio pulses where ONE wave holds 5400, so the replay
       was being sent as 3-5 waves with a socket round trip of milliseconds landing in the middle
       of frames. Every carrier sweep run through irReplay was therefore testing a broken
       transmission, in both directions - the one success and all the failures alike.
   IR: irRecord prints "first message only" and irTest.sh replays that. The later repeats in a
       capture are the corrupted ones anyway - a saturating receiver stretches marks and shrinks
       spaces until the long spaces carrying the 1 bits are eaten.
   IR: 56 kHz removed from every carrier list: one gree frame needs 5695 pulses at that frequency
       and can never be sent as a single wave. 44 kHz replaces it. The encoders greeIR/toshibaIR
       already refused to split, so plugin sends were never affected - only replays.
   IR: irTest.sh c <hz> <n> - sends n frames at one carrier alternating 21/25 C, so the ac's
       display counts the hits. One success proves nothing when the response is marginal.

v2022.191.258  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: the gree unit answered ONCE at 30 kHz and never at 38, which points at the carrier - the
       one property an ir RECEIVER cannot report, since it demodulates it away, so every
       recording and every loopback we made was blind to it. NOT yet a measurement: one success
       in one pass says as little as the five misses beside it. A marginal response makes the
       whole sweep unreliable in both directions, so nothing is ruled out until a frequency
       repeats.
   IR: the carrier is a property of the UNIT, so it is now one field per brand with its own
       default - TOSHIBA 38 kHz, GREE 30 kHz - selectable from 25 to 56 kHz, and the send path
       picks the one belonging to the brand being sent.
   IR: CARRIER SWEEP steps finely across the low end (25/26/28/30/32/33/34/36/38/40/56) instead
       of jumping 30 -> 33 -> 36. The first frequency that works is not necessarily the one with
       the most margin at the far end of the room, so run it twice from further away.

v2022.191.257  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: pi/irTest.sh - the record / play back test as two commands.  "irTest.sh a" records a real
       remote and saves the pulse list, "irTest.sh b" sends that same list back out on the led at
       30/33/36/38/40/56 kHz, 8 s apart. No sudo: the tools reach the gpios through the pigpiod
       socket, and killSudos kills anything holding "python" and "sudo".
   IR: the plugin's own irRecord call lost its sudo for the same reason - an echo run lasts about
       a minute and was being killed halfway, which orphaned the python child and left the pin
       claimed while no report ever came back. irRecord.py and irReplay.py added to killSudos'
       neverKill list as well, for hand started runs.
   IR: irRecord.py takes -h and its usage block now lists all six positional arguments with their
       defaults; the led gpio is range checked before pigpio sees it.

v2022.191.256  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: echo mode now pauses 15 s between the recording and the playback, and again before the
       de-biased repeat. The pause is the test: record an ON command (the ac obeys the remote,
       expected), press OFF on the remote during the pause, then our led replays the ON. Only
       the led can bring the unit back, so switching on IS the result - no shielding needed and
       no way to confuse it with the ac still obeying the button press. Countdown is printed,
       the menu states the timing in advance because the report only arrives when the run ends.
       Echo carrier frequency is selectable in the menu and passed through to irRecord.

v2022.191.255  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: irReplay v1.1 and irRecord echo take the carrier frequency as an argument. A replay
       reproduces the ENVELOPE only - the carrier was demodulated away by the receiver that made
       the recording and is generated fresh on playback - so replaying a real remote's capture
       sends its timing with OUR carrier. Varying the frequency is the point of the argument.
       greeIR: the inter-message gap is now measured from the end of the burst, so two repeats
       sit 40 ms apart like the remote instead of the 56 ms the extra sleep produced.

v2022.191.254  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: CARRIER FREQUENCY is now settable per device and sweepable. The carrier is the one
       property of a remote an ir RECEIVER cannot report - a TSOP demodulates it away and hands
       over only the envelope - so a recording can match a real remote pulse for pulse and still
       sit at the wrong frequency inside those marks, which an ac's narrow band receiver reads
       fine at 20 cm and ignores across the room. New "carrier frequency" field on the IR-AC
       device (30/33/36/38/40/56 kHz, 38 default), threaded through receiveCommands into both
       encoders, and named in the send log. CARRIER SWEEP button walks all six 7 s apart, each
       step asking for its own temperature, so the ac's display names the one that got through.
   IR: echo mode instructions corrected - the recording press has to be SHIELDED from the ac or
       the unit obeys the remote and the echo proves nothing.

v2022.191.253  @ 2026-09-01
   IR: ECHO MODE added to the remote recorder - "Record an IR remote (TSOP receiver).." can now
       retransmit each recording straight back out on the ir led, raw and again with the
       receiver's mark-stretching removed. This is the test for an ac that ignores a frame a
       receiver reads perfectly: the unit's OWN remote goes back out through our emitter with
       nothing decoded or rebuilt in between, so a reaction clears the emitter and the aim and
       blames the frame we build, and no reaction clears the frame and blames the light.
       irRecord.py v1.1 (gpio secs count ledGpio duty), receiveCommands runIrRecord passes the
       two new parameters, MenuItems gets the echo checkbox, led gpio and carrier duty.

==================================================
RELEASE  v2022.191.252  @ 2026-08-31
summary of everything that changed since v2022.191.171 - the per-version details follow below
==================================================

IN SHORT
 -  everything BLE runs in python now: scanning AND gatt. No hcidump / lescan / hcitool /
    gatttool / pybluez needed on a python3.5+ rPi
 -  ONE engine switch for all BLE work, plugin-wide and per rPi (was two separate settings that
    could contradict each other)
 -  BLE5 / extended advertising, ie RUUVI AIR E1 with the full data set (needs a BLE5 dongle)
 -  BLE radios are assigned to scan / connect / broadcast / extended-listener automatically by
    MEASURED quality - and each role can be pinned to a specific radio in the rpi device edit
 -  new menu tool "Qualify the BLE dongles of one RPI.." - measures what each adapter really
    delivers, nothing a dongle claims is trusted
 -  one device state per radio (hci0 .. hci3) instead of five states that each named the radio
    of one role
 -  beep is reliable and roughly twice as fast; battery reads follow the tags' wake-up order;
    single-dongle rPis can beep / read battery / drive switchbots now
 -  device states are optional: ruuvi value families, and min/max vs changes+trend separately,
    on every sensor
 -  the daily average is TIME-weighted now (was a plain average of the readings)
 -  device edit dialogs show rPi NAMES and GROUP NAMES, are sectioned, and 21 more device types
    can join groups
 -  the status column date is aligned by real pixel measurement and the column position is
    configurable
 -  about 25% fewer indigo state writes


FUNCTIONAL CHANGES  (new things, or things you can now set)

1.  BT5 EXTENDED ADVERTISING (BLE5) on the rPi - enables the Ruuvi Air E1 format with the FULL
    data set (PM 1.0/2.5/4.0/10, CO2, VOC, NOx, temp/hum/pressure). Needs a BLE5-capable USB
    dongle; pi onboard radios are "5.0"-branded but lack the feature. Many dongles report BLE5
    and deliver nothing, so the adapter is probed by an actual delivery test, the verdict is
    cached, and any failure falls back to legacy automatically instead of restart-looping.

2.  PYTHON BLE ENGINE EVERYWHERE. Two new stdlib-only modules do what external tools used to do:
    gattAttClient.py (GATT/ATT over LE L2CAP - beep, battery, switchbot, BLE sensor reads,
    xiaomi time-set, iphone/watch presence) and hciRawSocket.py (raw HCI scanning without
    pybluez). Both are the default. Important because gatttool and pybluez are gone or
    deprecated on current OS versions. rPis with python < 3.5 fall back to the old tools
    automatically.

3.  ONE ENGINE CHOICE instead of two. Plugin config: "BLE engine on the rPis - scanning +
    connecting" (python socket = default, or commandline tools). rPi device edit: "engine for
    scanning and connecting on THIS rPi" with "use the plugin-config default (recommended)".
    The two old prefs (rpiDataAcquistionmethod + BLEconnectmode) always meant the same
    decision - python or command-line tools - and could be set contradictingly. They still
    exist internally and the rPi-side parameters are unchanged; they are just derived from the
    one choice now. Migration: an install without the new pref keeps command-line ONLY if BOTH
    old prefs said so, everything else lands on python socket.

4.  AUTOMATIC RADIO ROLE ASSIGNMENT, deterministic and stable across reboots, by measured
    quality: scanning avoids the internal radio (worst receiver, shares the antenna with wifi),
    connecting avoids clone dongles (they scan fine but fail LE connects), the iBeacon
    broadcast rides along with the scanner, and a BLE5-capable dongle is reserved as the
    extended listener when there is another radio to scan with. With fewer radios everything
    runs legacy exactly as before.

5.  ROLE PINNING per radio - new section "Pin a role to a specific BLE radio" in the rpi device
    edit, one selector per radio the rPi actually reported. What may be picked follows the role
    strategy, not free choice: a USB radio offers only the scanner variants it was MEASURED to
    manage (BLE4 / BLE5 / BLE4+5), the internal radio offers only "BLEconnect + iphone" and only
    when there is a dongle to take the receive job, and every radio (except the last one) can be
    set to "ignore". Default everywhere is "let the RPi decide".

6.  NEW MENU ITEM "Qualify the BLE dongles of one RPI.." - measures per adapter: legacy scan
    rate, BT5 extended delivery, advertising, stability (bursts/silences), sensitivity (rssi),
    and optionally a real connect test against a tag - then says which piBeacon roles the dongle
    qualifies for (scan / broadcast / BLEconnect / extListener). Nothing a dongle CLAIMS is
    trusted, only delivered packets count. The results feed a catalogue in the indigo
    preferences directory that the role-pinning dialog reads. beaconloop and BLEconnect are
    paused while it runs.

7.  ONE DEVICE STATE PER RADIO: hci0 .. hci3 on the rpi device, eg
        58:11:22:53:8C:D5/USB/0b05:190e/UP/BLE4+5/scan4+5
    = mac / bus / usb-id / up-down / what it CAN do (measured) / what it IS doing
    (scan4, scan4+5, scan5, connect, BC, beep, or "-"). This REPLACES hciInfo, hciInfo_beacons,
    hciInfo_beep, hciInfo_BLEconnect and supportsBLE5 - five states that each named the radio of
    one role, so answering "what is hci1 doing" meant reading all five and cross-referencing
    macs. The mac comes first because it is fixed width: the four radios line up in the device
    list.

8.  SINGLE-DONGLE rPis support beep / battery / switchbot: BLEconnect shares the one radio with
    beaconloop and pauses it only for the job. BLEconnect also runs as a pure gatt service when
    no BLEconnect devices are configured.

9.  iPHONE / WATCH PRESENCE without pybluez and without hcitool - a stdlib backend does the
    classic page + rssi read.

10. RUUVI DEVICE STATES OPTIONAL PER FAMILY in device edit (temperature, humidity, pressure,
    CO2, VOC, illuminance, acceleration). Unchecking a family removes every state it owns
    including min/max/average/change/trend. A RuuviTag with all families off drops from 82
    states to 7. Everything defaults ON.

11. MIN/MAX AND CHANGED-VALUES ARE SEPARATE OPTIONS, on every device that has them (64 device
    types): "<state> - min/max/average" (12 states) and "<state> - changes + trend" (10 states)
    are two checkboxes instead of one. The two trend menus hang off the changes checkbox, which
    is what they configure. Both default ON.

12. STATUS COLUMN alignment is measured, not guessed: the status word is padded to a pixel
    budget using the real width of the 13pt system font, so the date part starts at the same
    place on every device. New plugin config "Status column: width of status part in pixels"
    (50 .. 150, default 80) moves the date column; changing it re-aligns EVERY existing device
    at once instead of waiting for each device to change on its own. Without PyObjC/AppKit in
    the plugin host the old per-word table is used and the setting has no effect (says so in
    the log).

13. DEVICE EDIT DIALOGS: rPi checkboxes are labelled with the rPi's indigo device NAME (ip as
    fallback), group checkboxes with the group's CONFIGURED NAME, both right-aligned. Group
    membership was added to 21 device types that never had it (all OUTPUT types, display,
    sprinkler, neopixel/sundial, vl6180x, 3 BLE types) and group "Other5" is reachable at last.
    342 section headers added and normalised to green "==== " headers. "state column" is called
    "status column" everywhere.

14. NEW STANDALONE rPi TOOLS (stdlib only): qualifyDongle.py (see 6), extScanTest.py (tests
    every adapter for extended advertising), ruuviPrint.py (prints every ruuvi in range with
    df3/df5/df6/E1 decoded).

15. NEW DEBUG OPTION (17) "update state lastUpdateFromRPI" - that state is about a quarter of
    all state writes, so it is off by default and the state then reads "off, to enable turn
    debug on". The value is still tracked internally, fast-down logic is unaffected.

16. INDIGO UPDATE LOAD down by roughly a quarter (item 15 plus TxPowerReceived only on a real
    change).


TECHNICAL CHANGES  (under the hood, nothing to set)

17. SOCKET is the default acquisition method: the rPi reads the HCI stream from a raw socket,
    no hcidump/lescan child processes, active scanning with scan responses (names/mfg_info).
    "Detect new beacon" captures from the live scan stream, so it no longer resets the adapter
    or blocks the main loop for 25 s.

18. STRUCTURE-BASED BEACON IDENTIFICATION (advMatch) runs in SHADOW MODE: knownBeaconTags.json
    carries an advMatch definition for 76 of 79 beacon types, matching on BLE AD sections
    instead of hex string + position. Both methods run in parallel, the old string method still
    decides, differences are logged as ADVMATCH-DIFF. After a clean burn-in the string method
    can be retired. Backup: knownBeaconTags.json.bak-preAdvMatch

19. SHELL CALLS REPLACED BY PYTHON in the rPi programs: rm / mkdir / cp / touch / chmod / chown /
    "echo > file" went through subprocess with shell=True, swallowed their errors and could not
    report what failed. There are helpers now (removeFile, copyFile, makeDir, touchFile,
    setMode, setOwner, makeAccessible, writeFileAsRoot, runShellBatch, procList), simple files
    are written atomically (tmp + rename) so a reader never sees a truncated file, and process
    checks read /proc instead of parsing ps output.

20. LOG FILE OWNERSHIP: a helper started with sudo CREATED /var/log/pibeacon as root, and every
    later program running as the pi user could then not append to it - python logging swallows
    that error silently, so the log just stopped. The permissions are fixed at startup by every
    program that touches the log, and temp/ is made pi-owned after master (root) recreates it.

21. APT NO LONGER FIGHTS PACKAGES THE OS DOES NOT HAVE: "E: Unable to locate package X" is not
    a transient failure - the package is gone or renamed in that release (libgpiod2 became
    libgpiod3 in trixie), and retrying it on every start burned an apt run and filled the log
    with the same error forever. Such names are remembered in aptNotAvailable.txt and skipped;
    delete that file to retry after an OS upgrade.

22. rPi LOGGING: timestamps carry tenths of a second, the redundant "Lv:xx" column is gone, and
    the rPi programs log at level 20 (they used to log routine messages at 30 = warning).

23. PLOT SCRIPTS no longer flood the indigo log: basicConfig sets the ROOT logger, so every
    matplotlib and PIL module propagated its DEBUG chatter into the plugin log ("Loaded backend
    Agg version v2.2.", findfont scores). Silencing single sub-loggers never worked because the
    logger is "matplotlib.pyplot", not "pyplot" - the PARENT logger covers the whole tree in one
    line. Affects makeBeaconPositionPlots.py, makeCameraPlot.py, makeLidar360Plot.py.

24. plugin.py: sorted into 13 labelled sections, ~900 lines of static tables moved to
    piBeaconConstants.py, the rpi-selection and group-membership <Field> blocks are generated by
    getDeviceConfigUiXml instead of being copy-pasted into every device type (Devices.xml shrank
    by ~4,300 lines), 1804 -> 24 .format() calls are f-strings, cProfile support removed. Every
    f-string conversion was verified against python 3.11 (what Indigo runs) as well as 3.13.
    Property names are unchanged, nothing migrates.

25. Cleanup pass over beaconloop.py and BLEconnect.py removed several hundred unused
    declarations and dead variables; pi/*.py stay python-2 compatible.

    program versions: plugin 2022.191.248, beaconloop v21.2, BLEconnect v12.0,
                      gattAttClient v1.4, qualifyDongle v2.4


FIXES

26. CRASH on updated bookworm/newer kernels: kernels with the 2024 setsockopt validation require
    a 16-byte HCI filter struct; the 14-byte filter every tool has sent for years (pybluez
    included) is rejected. Also the crash-restart used /usr/bin/python, which does not exist on
    py3-only systems, so a crashed beaconloop stayed dead there.

27. SOCKET METHOD, years-old wrong opcode: the LE scan PARAMETERS (active scan) were sent to
    SET_SCAN_ENABLE instead of SET_SCAN_PARAMETERS, so the controller stayed in its default
    PASSIVE mode and never received scan responses - which is why the socket method missed
    messages with names/mfg_info. Plus: quiet periods no longer restart the program, all
    kernel-buffered packets are drained per read, scan re-enable re-sends the parameters (a
    hciconfig reset reverts to passive defaults), and stack restarts no longer leak the socket.

28. RESTART CASCADES on long-running rPis: a single junk-length frame aborted a whole message
    set and triggered the restart ladder; the no-data clock was not reset after a stack restart;
    the restart counter was per lifetime, so an isolated hiccup hours later escalated
    immediately.

29. DONGLE ROULETTE after an rPi reboot: with "let the RPi decide" the pinned adapter was
    DISCARDED on every restart. The pin is only invalidated when an explicit UART/USB
    restriction conflicts with it; the hci name is always resolved FROM the pinned mac (hci
    numbers change between boots, the mac is the dongle identity).

30. qualifyDongle LEFT THE RADIOS PAUSED: the tool kept running after printing its report
    (piBeaconUtils' send thread is not a daemon, so python waited for it at shutdown) while the
    caller's keep-alive thread refreshed the pause files every 5 s - defeating both failsafes.
    Now: 600 s hard cap on the pause, a bounded wait that reads a FILE instead of a pipe (a pipe
    stays open as long as any inherited fd lives), and the tool flushes its queue and exits.
    Both pause files could also never expire, because an unreadable or half-written file fell
    back to "now" and so always looked freshly refreshed - they fall back to the file's mtime
    now.

31. qualifyDongle CONNECT TEST measured nothing: it paged tags as BDADDR_LE_PUBLIC (0x01) while
    beacon tags use static RANDOM addresses (0x02), so every attempt on every radio timed out
    and read as broken hardware. The type is derived from the address now (can be forced with
    addrType=public|random) and is printed in the report. The role verdict follows the
    measurement, not the ACL MTU guess: 100% grants "connect" whatever the MTU says, 0%
    withholds it whatever the MTU says.

32. SWITCHBOT CONTACT phantom triggers (~2 per 20 minutes with nobody pressing anything):
    type-4 frames have NO button counter - nibble 13 there is a 2-bit rotating OPEN indicator.
    It was read as a full nibble, sent to indigo as "buttonCounter" and written over the REAL
    counter from the type-3 frame, so every occurrence produced a wrong value and then the
    correct one a second later = two state changes, two triggers. Type 3 is the only source now.

33. DAILY AVERAGE was a plain sum/count of the readings, which is only correct when readings are
    evenly spaced in time - and they are not, because a sensor reports on value-CHANGE or on the
    idle timer, so a short hectic period outweighed a long calm one. Example with the shipped
    defaults: 20 hours at 10 C then 4 hours at 20 C produced AveToday 17.5 C where the true
    average is 11.7 C. AveToday/AveYesterday are TIME-WEIGHTED now; the accumulator travels with
    changedValues.json and is rebuilt from the clock after a restart.

34. TREND was written without checking whether the state exists. The KeyError was swallowed
    silently - and that also skipped writing the value history back, so the whole
    change-over-time calculation quietly stopped updating. Also, the changed-values loop tested
    a property built from the FULL state name (eg "TemperatureChangeHours01_EnableMinMax"),
    which never exists, so that check had always been a no-op.

35. BEEP latency and reliability (roughly 12 s down to 4-8 s), mainly by keeping the LE scan
    running during a connect - the kernel manages the scan around create-connection itself - and
    by using shorter, re-issued connection windows. Connect failures are stage-tagged with the
    adapter name. BATTERY READS follow the order tags actually WAKE UP instead of a blind queue
    order, so sleeping tags no longer burn listen windows.

36. piMAC reported the SCANNER's mac instead of the iBeacon TRANSMITTER's, breaking the rpi
    device link and rpi-to-rpi online detection whenever the roles were split.

37. REPEATED FULL PARAMETER REPROCESS on the rPi: a timestamp stamped on every parameters build
    made any resend look new, forcing a rebuild of program/sensor lists every cycle. Fixed on
    both sides - the plugin does not stamp it, and the rPi ignores that field when comparing.

38. beacon_parameters GROWING WITHOUT LIMIT: after a parameter rename (acceptNewiBeacons ->
    acceptNewBeaconsMinSIgnal) the "accept new beacons" default reverted to accept-ALL. The
    default is OFF now. An already-bloated file still needs menu -> ignore beacons + reset
    history to shrink.

39. validateDeviceConfigUi_beacon indexed valuesDict["rPiEnable<n>"] directly for every pi, so
    one missing key raised a KeyError and killed the whole validation.

40. PM states were Integer, truncating the 0.1 ug/m3 resolution (1.2 became 1) - they are Real
    now.

41. COIN-CELL BATTERY PERCENT was far too low in the cold: coin cells sag about 12 mV/C below
    room temperature and the whole voltage curve moves down, but only the empty point was being
    corrected. Warm behaviour is unchanged.

42. RUUVI AIR matched to the actual hardware: battery states removed (it is USB-C powered) and
    Illuminance defaults OFF (Ruuvi dropped the light sensor). The checkbox remains.

43. Illuminance on SHELLY devices now goes through the standard state writer, so its
    min/max/average/change/trend states are finally filled instead of staying empty.

44. i2c GHOST ADDRESSES: scanning while another program used the bus made a clock-stretching
    device ack a whole address range. The bus is scanned twice and only addresses seen in both
    passes are kept.

45. BEACON NAMES with umlauts or other multi-byte UTF-8 characters were mangled.

46. iPHONE PRESENCE: false "away" on multi-adapter rPis (the page went via the default adapter),
    flapping on half-built connections, an absent phone counting as an adapter error and
    restarting BLEconnect, and configured refresh times being ignored (phones were paged every
    main-loop pass).

47. The per-rPi verbose debug switch in the plugin config did nothing - the rPi read a different
    key than the plugin sent.

48. A stale gattAttClient.py from a partial file send is detected and reported at startup
    instead of silently missing fixes.

49. DISPLAY STOPPED ITSELF ON BOOT: the stop marker temp/display.stop was written
    unconditionally, so it was lying around on a fresh boot and the display master started a
    moment later read it and exited ("stop was requested"). It is written only when a display is
    actually running, and removed once it has done its job.

50. The read-only-filesystem check ran "echo x > x" and grepped the output, which left a file
    literally named "x" in the working directory on every healthy boot. It writes a temp file
    and looks at the errno now.

51. ROUTINE LOG LINES that fired on every config save, every delayed rPi action and every device
    start ("adding isMememberOfChangedValues") are behind their debug areas.


NOTES / UPGRADE

52. The switchbot Remote does NOT broadcast button presses - the advertisement payload is
    immutable and presses only wake it to talk to its paired bot/curtain. Battery reading and
    beep-to-locate work. Use a ShellyButton if button broadcasts are needed.
53. A PLUGIN RELOAD is required for the state-type and state-list changes (PM states Integer ->
    Real; rpi devices lose hciInfo* / supportsBLE5 and gain hci0 .. hci3).
54. The MEANING of AveToday/AveYesterday changed (see 33) - triggers or charts tuned to the old
    sample average will read differently on volatile sensors.
55. The optional-states options (10, 11) all default ON, so existing devices are unchanged until
    they are edited and saved.
56. When the plugin bundle is replaced manually or via a sync tool instead of the Indigo
    installer, reload the plugin afterwards.

Added features / changes in added features / changes in v2022.191.252 @ 2026-08-31

IN SHORT
 -  GREE air conditioners are supported: greeIR.py, built from a real remote, not a datasheet
 -  the IR device is brand independent now: OUTPUT-Thermostat-IR-AC with an "AC brand" setting
 -  one menu item sends any AC command; three actions cover fan, mode, louvers and the buttons
 -  irRecord names the protocol it heard and decodes toshiba AND gree

DETAILS
 -  DEVICE RENAMED: type id OUTPUTthermostatToshibaIR -> OUTPUTthermostatIRac, shown as
    OUTPUT-Thermostat-IR-AC, wire command "toshibaIR" -> "irAC" (the rpi still answers the old
    name). EXISTING TOSHIBA DEVICES MUST BE RECREATED - indigo keys them by the type id
 -  greeIR.py: 8 byte state, sent LSB first as 32 bits + a 3 bit footer + ~20 ms + 32 bits,
    header 9000/4500. Every field was measured off the remote with irRecord and the self test
    rebuilds 24 recorded states byte for byte: power (its own bit, unlike toshiba where off is a
    mode), fan auto/1/2/3, modes cool/dry/fan/heat, temperature = byte1 + 16, both louvers with
    all their positions, swing-auto, sleep, health, light, and the checksum
 -  gree oddities worth knowing, all measured: in DRY mode byte1 is the HUMIDITY setting, not a
    temperature (the humidity button does nothing at all in cool mode); the sleep button also
    drops the fan to 1; swing-auto and the "auto" louver position are INDEPENDENT bits even
    though one press set both; byte2 bit 6 is HEALTH, which the reference calls ModelA
 -  a "mode 6" appeared twice with a VALID checksum and was still wrong - gree's checksum is 4
    bits, so one corrupted frame in sixteen passes. Clean captures only; the remote and the app
    both show five modes
 -  irRecord: detects and names TOSHIBA / GREE, decodes both, reads multi-block protocols, shows
    msb and lsb, records a SEQUENCE of presses and diffs them, trims noise before the header, and
    says when the repeats of one transmission disagree (a reception error, not the remote)
 -  irReplay.py: sends a recorded pulse list back out unchanged
 -  menu: "Send an IR-AC command.." - one item, an IR-AC device selector, and mode / fan /
    temperature lists that follow that device's brand (toshiba 1..5 and 17..30 C, gree 1..3 and
    16..30 C), plus louvers, sleep, health and light
 -  actions: "IR-AC: set fan speed", "set mode", "set louvers (swing)", "set sleep / health /
    light". Each stores the choice on the device and resends the COMPLETE state - one IR frame
    carries mode, temperature and fan together, so there is no sending "just the fan"
 -  menu section "manage sensor devices" renamed to "manage devices"; the IR items live there
 -  sendToshibaIRState renamed sendIRacState - it builds either brand now

Added features / changes in added features / changes in v2022.191.251 @ 2026-08-30

IN SHORT
 -  the toshiba IR command reached the AC for the first time: two wave_add_generic calls were
    building ONE scrambled waveform instead of two frames
 -  irRecord.py: record an IR remote with a TSOP/1838 receiver, decode it, print it to the log
 -  irReplay.py: send a recorded pulse list back out - the tool that found the bug
 -  message form (9 or 10 byte), byte-7 spare bit and carrier duty are device settings now

DETAILS
 -  ROOT CAUSE of an AC that ignored frames whose bytes were provably identical to its own
    remote's: pigpio builds a waveform by ABSOLUTE TIME OFFSET - that is what lets one wave carry
    independent signals for several gpios. A second wave_add_generic therefore does NOT continue
    where the first ended, it starts again at time 0 and MERGES. The chunking added earlier that
    day to stay under the 64 kB socket limit split the two-frame burst across two calls, so frame
    2 was ORed on top of frame 1: carrier where the gaps belong, marks running together, spaces
    gone. Every command sends two frames, so every command was scrambled - while every single
    frame test came out clean, which is what made it so hard to see.
    Now: ONE FRAME = ONE WAVE = ONE CALL (~3770 pulses = 45 kB, inside both limits), with the
    inter-frame gap appended to the wave it follows so it stays DMA timed. sendFrame refuses a
    pulse list too big for one call instead of splitting it.
 -  measured on hardware afterwards: 3 leds at 80 mA reach an AC anywhere in the room, pointed at
    it or away - reflections are enough. ONE led needs to be aimed, and then works at ~3 m
 -  irRecord.py + menu item "Record an IR remote (TSOP receiver)..": waits for a button press,
    decodes header/bit timings, frame count and bytes, checks both toshiba checksums and says
    whether it is the protocol toshibaIR builds. Records a SEQUENCE of presses and diffs them, so
    "which bits carry the temperature" is answered by pressing up/down/up/down
 -  irReplay.py: transmits a recorded pulse list unchanged. Replaying a remote's own recording
    through our led is what separated "the frame is wrong" from "the chain is wrong"
 -  toshibaIR: message form selectable (9 byte / 10 byte), the byte-7 spare bit that a real
    RAS-G2KVP remote sets (F2 0D 03 FC 01 10 01 80 90 vs the library's ..00 10) and the carrier
    duty cycle, all as device settings
 -  eco / hi power used to overwrite the checksum byte in the 9 byte message - they need the
    10 byte form, and now force it
 -  device edit: TEST LED (blinks the led for a phone camera) and TEST VARIANTS (tries all 8
    protocol variants, on/off, so the AC itself says which one it answers)

STATIC AUDIT of the whole plugin (uninitialised variables, format strings, xml wiring, duplicates)
 -  piBeaconUtils getOsVersion was defined TWICE. The second definition silently won: it shelled
    out to cat, did not cache, returned 0 instead of 8 when VERSION_ID was missing, and raised
    IndexError on an unquoted VERSION_ID=13 (split('="')[1]). Removed; the surviving one reads
    /etc/os-release directly instead of through cat
 -  piBeaconUtils checkIfwebserverINPUTrunning was defined twice as well - the dead first copy
    tested webserverSTATUS.py, ie the WRONG program. Removed
 -  plugin startTcpipListening returned a local socS that was only ever bound INSIDE the retry
    loop: a port that stayed busy for all 90 tries ended in NameError instead of the intended
    "tcpip stack did not load" message. Initialised to None
 -  get_raspi_config.py: print("... err={}",format(e)) - a COMMA where the dot belongs, so the
    message printed the placeholder and the value side by side
 -  BLEconnect.py: a log line with a {} placeholder and nothing to fill it
 -  Devices.xml: OUTPUTi2cRelay and OUTPUTgpio-1-ONoff had enableBindingId="stateDone" on four
    fields, but neither dialog has a stateDone field - pointed at piDone, which is what the
    visibleBindingId on the very same fields uses
 -  simplei2csensors / tmp006 / tmp007: readS16BE defined twice with an identical body, removed
 -  checked and clean: every CallbackMethod and list method named in the xml exists in plugin.py,
    all five xml files parse, no duplicate device/action/menu ids, no duplicate field ids inside a
    dialog, no name used before its first assignment anywhere in plugin.py or the 104 pi programs
